Every Airline Flying From The US To Australia & The Routes Served In 2026

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Global Guy & Kevin Hackert | Shutterstock, Simple Flying

Nonstop travel between the US and Australia remains one of the most important long-haul markets across the Pacific, connecting major US gateways with Australia's largest cities and several destinations beyond its traditional east coast hubs. In 2026, airlines are operating an extensive selection of services, with the US gateways of Los Angeles International Airport (LAX), San Francisco International Airport (SFO), and Dallas/Fort Worth International Airport (DFW) providing the majority of connections.

The market features a combination of established nonstop routes, competitive services, and newer connections designed to give travelers greater flexibility. There are four main airlines providing nonstop links between the two countries, with a total of 8,820 flights scheduled between them in 2026, according to the latest data from ch-aviation. This equates to a total of 2,610,162 seats, and more than 18 billion available seat miles across the various airline and route combinations. These totals illustrate the scale of the market, while individual routes reveal how airlines distribute capacity according to demand, network strategy, and the importance of their respective US gateways.

American Airlines serves Australia through a focused network linking Los Angeles International Airport and Dallas/Fort Worth International with Sydney Airport (SYD) and Brisbane Airport (BNE). The airline's scheduled services for 2026 include 365 flights between Los Angeles and Sydney, and 135 between Dallas/Fort Worth and Brisbane. The oneworld carrier also schedules 13 flights between Los Angeles and Brisbane, giving it a presence across several important transpacific city pairs rather than relying on a single gateway. Sydney Kingsford Smith Airport (SYD) is the most prominent Australian destination in American Airlines' published network, with the Los Angeles service offering a major link between Southern California and Australia's largest international aviation market.

The Dallas/Fort Worth to Brisbane route provides another important option, particularly for travelers originating in the central and eastern US who can connect through American Airlines' major Texas hub. These services complement the airline's broader domestic network, allowing passengers from numerous US cities to travel to Australia without necessarily beginning their journeys on the West Coast. The airline's schedule also demonstrates the different scales of its individual routes, with Los Angeles to Sydney recording 110,960 seats and Dallas/Fort Worth to Brisbane offering 35,031. Its Los Angeles to Brisbane operation is substantially smaller, with 3,705 seats. American Airlines' network between the US and Australia in 2026 is summarized in the table below:

Dallas/Fort Worth International Airport (DFW)

Delta Air Lines operates an extensive selection of services connecting Los Angeles with Sydney, Brisbane, and Melbourne. The schedule shows 418 flights between Los Angeles and Sydney and 182 between Los Angeles and Melbourne. The SkyTeam carrier's 65 flights between Los Angeles and Brisbane add another option for passengers traveling between the US and Australia's east coast. Los Angeles to Sydney is Delta Air Lines' largest Australian route, accounting for 114,981 seats and 860,862,747 available seat miles. The Los Angeles to Melbourne service adds 50,050 seats, creating a substantial second connection to Australia. These routes give Delta Air Lines a significant presence in the transpacific market, while its Los Angeles connections allow passengers from other parts of the US to access its long-haul services.

Delta Air Lines' network emphasizes established destinations with strong demand for business and leisure travel. Sydney provides access to Australia's largest metropolitan area, while Melbourne attracts visitors interested in its cultural attractions, sporting events, and business districts. Although the airline's Brisbane operation is smaller than its Sydney and Melbourne services, it broadens the available choices for travelers heading to Queensland. Together, these routes demonstrate how Delta Air Lines combines its US network with demand across Australia's principal international gateways.

The airport handles millions of passengers annually.

Qantas has a particularly important role in the US to Australia market as Australia's flag carrier, with services connecting several American cities to Sydney, Melbourne, and Brisbane. The carrier's operation include 353 flights between Los Angeles and Sydney, 357 between Los Angeles and Melbourne, and 336 between Los Angeles and Brisbane. Qantas also operates nonstop services between San Francisco and Sydney, demonstrating that its US network extends beyond its principal Los Angeles gateway. The carrier's Los Angeles to Melbourne route offers 105,666 seats, while Los Angeles to Brisbane offers 78,740 seats. Qantas' Los Angeles to Sydney service provides 170,458 seats, making it one of the most substantial individual routes between the two countries. The airline's San Francisco to Sydney operation adds another important connection for passengers traveling from Northern California, reducing the need to travel through Los Angeles before beginning a transpacific journey. The oneworld carrier's route network is outlined in the table below:

Qantas' route structure reflects the importance of connecting Australia's major population centers with the US, particularly for travelers visiting family, pursuing business opportunities, or taking extended holidays. Sydney and Melbourne serve as major international gateways, while Brisbane provides access to Queensland and its coastal tourism destinations. With multiple US departure points and several Australian arrivals, Qantas offers a network that serves both point-to-point travelers and passengers connecting through larger domestic and international networks.

United Airlines has a broad network of transpacific services, with routes from San Francisco, Los Angeles, and Houston to destinations ranging from Sydney and Melbourne to Brisbane and Adelaide. Its schedule includes 443 flights between San Francisco and Sydney, 362 between Los Angeles and Sydney, and 363 between San Francisco and Melbourne.

San Francisco to Sydney is the largest United Airlines route to Australia, with a total of 115,855 seats and 859,296,535 available seat miles scheduled for 2026. The Los Angeles to Sydney route contributes another 93,034 seats, while San Francisco to Melbourne adds 93,291. United Airlines also serves Sydney from Houston, with 154 flights and 37,163 seats, providing a useful alternative for travelers originating in the southern US:

The Star Alliance carrier's extensive US domestic network helps support these long-haul services by connecting passengers from cities across the country to its international gateways. San Francisco offers convenient access for West Coast travelers, while Los Angeles provides another major departure point and Houston expands the network into Texas. The airline's San Francisco to Adelaide Airport (ADL) connection is particularly noteworthy because it extends the available Australian destination choices beyond the country's more established eastern gateways.

It is the largest unserved market between Australia & the US. Find out more here!

Los Angeles to Sydney stands out as one of the defining routes in the US to Australia market, with American Airlines, Delta Air Lines, Qantas, and United Airlines all represented. Their individual schedules show 365, 418, 353, and 362 flights, respectively, giving a combined total of 1,498 flights across these four carriers in 2026. Delta Air Lines records the highest individual flight count, although all four airlines contribute substantial capacity. The route's importance reflects the strong connections between Southern California and Australia's largest city, supported by tourism, business travel, education, and visiting friends and relatives.

Los Angeles is also a major connecting hub for passengers arriving from elsewhere in the US, while Sydney offers onward connections to destinations throughout Australia and the wider region. The combination makes the route attractive to both travelers beginning their journeys in either city and those connecting through the respective airline networks. Competition between four major carriers gives passengers a broader choice of schedules, loyalty programs, onboard products, and connecting itineraries.

A total of 110,960 seats are due to be operated by American Airlines throughout the year, with 114,981 by Delta Air Lines, 170,458 by Qantas, and 93,034 by United Airlines on their respective Los Angeles to Sydney services. These figures should be read alongside the flight counts, since aircraft capacity and scheduling patterns can influence the number of seats available. The four airlines operate a mix of Boeing 787-9, Airbus A350-900, and Airbus A380 aircraft on this route, and collectively, the services underline the route's central position in transpacific aviation.

United Airlines' San Francisco to Adelaide connection is a noteworthy addition to the range of routes linking the US with Australia, as Adelaide has traditionally received less direct transpacific service than Sydney or Melbourne. The carrier will operate a total of 44 flights and 11,308 seats on this route throughout 2026. Although considerably smaller than the established Los Angeles - Sydney operation, the service offers a direct link to South Australia rather than requiring passengers to arrive at another Australian city first. Adelaide provides access to a different side of Australia, with attractions including its central market, nearby wine regions, coastal scenery, and cultural festivals. The city is also an important gateway for visitors exploring South Australia, including the Barossa Valley and the Fleurieu Peninsula. For US travelers, a nonstop connection can also reduce the need for an additional domestic flight within Australia, although recent reports suggest the services are far from full, leading to a reduction in flights for the rest of 2026.

This route broadens United Airlines' Australian network by adding another destination beyond the country's largest eastern cities. Its relatively limited capacity suggests a smaller operation than the airline's established Sydney and Melbourne services, but the connection provides another option for travelers with business, family, or leisure interests in South Australia. The route's longer-term significance will depend on passenger demand, operating frequency, and how effectively United Airlines connects Adelaide with its wider US network.

Pope Leo sets off on 12-day South America tour

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The pontiff will hold meetings and celebrate Mass in Uruguay, Argentina and Peru, with the latter attracting particular interest. The trip will be the longest and farthest of his papacy so far.

The plane carrying Pope Leo XIV to South America for the beginning of his almost two-week tour set off from Rome on Friday.

Following a 13-and-a-half-hour flight, the pontiff is set to land in Uruguay, where he will spend three days before heading on to Argentina, and finally Peru.

The 71-year-old US-born pope is recovering from a lung cancer surgery that took place just three weeks ago.

Latin America is home to around 40% of the world's 1.4 billion Catholics, for whom the pope is their spiritual leader. However, fewer and fewer people on the continent identify as Catholic.

Pope Leo's first leg of the tour takes him to Uruguay, the most secular Latin American country.

It was the first country in the world to legalize recreational marijuana use and has since legalized abortion, same-sex marriage and assisted dying, all against opposition from the Catholic Church.

However, the enthusiasm around Leo's visit has come as a bit of a surprise, with tickets for his prayer vigil at Montevideo's Centenario Stadium selling out in just two days.

The trip will begin with a meeting with President Yamandu Orsi, leader of a center-left alliance. He is also planning to visit the Casavalle neighborhood, which is notorious for its high rates of poverty and violence.

With the first papal visit to Uruguay since 1988, Leo will be hoping to reinvigorate the faith in a country where only a fraction of believers attend Mass.

On Sunday, Leo will cross over to neighboring Argentina, the home of his papal predecessor Francis, who, however, disappointed his native compatriots by never returning home as pope.

At the beginning of Francis's papacy, some 71% of Argentinians identified as Catholic, according to the Pew Research Center. By 2024, this had fallen to 58%.

Leo is set to meet with Argentinian President Javier Milei, who has come under criticism from the Catholic Church for his sweeping cuts that have hampered programs for the poor and needy.

He is also planning to meet with business leaders, trade unions, workers and social representatives.

The final leg of the trip, on Wednesday, will see Leo return to Peru, where he lived and preached for two decades.

There is excitement about his visit to Peru, with the pope planning to celebrate Mass in his old diocese of Chiclayo as well as in the mountain village of Santa Cruz de Succhabamba.

The pope will also celebrate Mass in the former Incan capital city of Cusco before honoring Peru's indigenous population by traveling to Pucallpa in the Peruvian Amazon.

The excitement can be seen in the street vendors selling T-shirts and rosaries with his likeness, while bakeries have been selling Leo bread.

Peru has also seen a weakening of the faithful in recent years, with the Catholic population falling from 76% in 2013-2014 to around 67% now.

Transfer news LIVE: Arsenal eye triple deal; huge Man Utd manager twist; Chelsea, Liverpool, Tottenham latest

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The January window is fast approaching as Arsenal, Chelsea, Manchester United, Liverpool and Tottenham all pursue some huge new mid-season signings to help reach their goals. Arsenal are said to be targeting a move for Antonio Nusa as they also eye Eli Junior Kroupi and Kenan Yildiz, with the likes of Alex Baena also believed to be in Mikel Arteta’s sights along with Julian Alvarez.

Didier Deschamps has reportedly turned down the chance to manage Manchester United, where Sunday’s game against Aston Villa will be crucial to determining the future of under-fire boss Michael Carrick. Tottenham are understood to still retain matching rights for Harry Kane after the England captain signed a record-breaking new Bayern Munich contract that is believed to make him the highest-paid player in Europe in terms of base salary.

Chelsea are confident of signing Alex Scott when the market reopens in the New Year, while Liverpool are keen on a swoop for Mexico wonderkid Gilberto Mora and Lamine Camara as Trent Alexander-Arnold continues to be linked with a sensational switch back to Anfield. Follow all the latest transfer news, rumours and gossip live below!

Real Madrid look to have won the race for Manchester United sensation JJ Gabriel.

The teenage star shocked Old Trafford chiefs back in September by stating his desire to leave the club immediately, just days before he was expected to make his senior debut aged just 15.

All of the rest of the heavyweight clubs across Europe were inevitably interested in signing one of the brightest youth talents in England, but Real Madrid and Barcelona quickly emerged as the front runners.

Madrid were said to be leading the race, with Fabrizio Romano now reporting that Gabriel has chosen the Bernabeu and that his camp have informed all parties of his decision.

The move is expected to be sealed soon, with Man Utd likely only due minimal training compensation.

Tottenham retain matching rights on Harry Kane despite his new Bayern Munich contract, reports claim.

The England captain yesterday put pen to paper on a lucrative contract extension in Bavaria until 2029 that is said to make him the highest-paid player in Europe in terms of base salary.

That bumper new deal does not impact Tottenham’s first-option right to match any future bids for Kane, per The Telegraph.

That agreement was reached during his initial exit from Spurs in 2023 and is said to lie outside of his contract, so has been unaffected.

Didier Deschamps has reportedly told Manchester United that he is not interested in taking over as manager at Old Trafford.

Michael Carrick’s future as Man Utd boss remains hugely uncertain ahead of Sunday’s crunch home Premier League clash with Aston Villa, which is likely to prove pivotal.

Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann had previously been considered as the leading candidates for the role if United - who will not consider an interim solution this time - were to sack Carrick, though FootMercato in France reported on Thursday that the club had also approached former long-time Les Bleus head coach Deschamps.

However, he is said to have told them that he currently has no interest in the job in another blow to the beleaguered Red Devils.

Arsenal are tracking Antonio Nusa ahead of the January transfer window, reports claim.

The Gunners are known to be keen on attacking reinforcements when the market reopens for business in the New Year and have reportedly had their eyes on Bournemouth forward Eli Junior Kroupi and Juventus winger Kenan Yildiz.

However, The Sun say that Arsenal are also keeping close tabs on former Club Brugge star Nusa, who continues to impress for both RB Leipzig and Norway.

Could a lucrative triple deal yet be in the offing at the Emirates Stadium after a somewhat disappointing summer?

Calciomercato claims that Juventus are considering a January move for Manchester United striker Benjamin Sesko despite his injury.

The Italian club have a €100million transfer budget following a capital injection and are assessing several attacking targets.

Folarin Balogun is also under consideration as Juventus explore options to strengthen their forward line.

Reports in Spain say that Barcelona have identified Arsenal defender Piero Hincapie as a potential signing for the January transfer window.

Sporting director Deco has made initial contact with the 24-year-old's representatives to explore a possible move.

Hincapie has struggled for regular minutes, but Mikel Arteta is reluctant to let players leave Arsenal in January.

Teamtalk claims that Manchester United have intensified their interest in Como defender Jacobo Ramon ahead of the 2027 transfer windows.

The 21-year-old is valued at around €50million, with Chelsea also keen and Arsenal, Liverpool and Fulham monitoring him.

Real Madrid hold a 50% sell-on clause and buy-back options, with a potential return to Spain under consideration.

Fichjaes claims that Barcelona have identified Manchester City left-back Rayan Ait-Nouri as a potential January signing.

The 25-year-old has made just three appearances this season, with City valuing him at around €30million.

Barca could pursue a deal if Alejandro Balde leaves, while Manchester United are also interested.

Unai Emery will not be going to Manchester United.

The former Arsenal and PSG boss is said to be a “dream target” for Old Trafford chiefs in the event that they sack Michael Carrick.

However, The Telegraph report that Emery is in advanced talks over signing a new long-term contract at Villa Park.

Villa said yesterday that Emery had been involved in an “important working meeting to plan the club's future and lay the foundations for its long-term sporting and institutional project”.

That meeting - in which they discussed committing to joint development beyond 2029 under Emery’s leadership - also involved chairman Nassef Sawiris, president of football operations Roberto Olabe and director of football operations Damian Vidagany.

Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international duty with Portugal.

The country’s greatest-ever player announced his decision via a video to camera on his social media channels.

Ronaldo netted 146 goals in 234 games for Portugal, becoming the all-time leading scorer in the history of men’s international football.

The striker was suspended by the Portuguese FA last month after he stormed out of the Nations League camp in October, having fallen out with manager Jorge Jesus.

Malaysia: Jailed ex-leader Najib to leave prison for house arrest

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Convicted in the 1MDB scandal for embezzling millions, jailed former Prime Minister Najib Razak will serve his prison term under house arrest after his party had collected donations to pay his fine.

Malaysia's graft-convicted former Prime Minister Najib Razak is set to leave prison to sit out the remainder of a six-year sentence under house arrest, after his political party paid a 50 million ringgit ($12.3 million, €11 million) fine.

Najib's party, United Malays National Organization (UMNO), collected sufficient funds through a donation drive, Asyraf Wajid Dusuki, the party secretary-general, said in a statement on Friday.

Although it was not immediately clear when Najib would be officially allowed to leave prison, the AFP news agency said a report sa him in a car driving away on Friday. Police were stationed at his house in Kuala Lumpur early Friday and the road leading to his home was blocked.

Najib, 73, has been serving a six-year prison sentence for his role in the 1MDB corruption scandal, named after 1Malaysia Development Berhad, a state investment fund he co-founded while serving as prime minister from 2009 to 2018.

In 2020, Najib was originally sentenced to 12 years in prison for abuse of power, criminal breach of trust and money laundering involving $10.3 million funneled into his bank accounts. He began his sentence in 2022.

Malaysia's king, Sultan Ibrahim, granted Najib a rare royal clemency in September, subject to the payment of 50 million Malaysian ringgit, allowing him to serve the rest of his sentence under house arrest.

The house arrest applies only to his reduced six-year sentence in the first trial.

It doesn't cover a separate 15-year prison sentence imposed in December 2025 for corruption and money laundering in a second trial tied to the 1MDB scandal.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Najib is appealing that conviction, which saw him fined 11.4 billion ringgit.

If he fails to win the appeal, he could go back to prison after August 23, 2028.

The scandal sparked national outrage in Malaysia and led to the 2018 election defeat of Najib's party United Malays National Organization (UNMO), which governed the country since independence from Britain in 1957.

In spite of the embezzlement, Najib still enjoys political support and is influential in this party, which is part of the government that came to power in 2022.

Hyperliquid (HYPE) Volatility Might Surge as the Biggest Exchange Still Limits Withdrawals

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Binance has announced the second round of withdrawal reopenings, but Hyperliquid's HYPE token remains missing from the list, leaving holders unable to withdraw the asset for eight consecutive days, broadly speaking.

On November 6, Binance published its 9th recovery update, verifying that withdrawals for more cryptocurrencies would begin reopening at 04:00 UTC, broadly speaking; the announcement covers dozens of assets across the nine blockchain networks, which includes BNB Smart Chain, Ethereum, Base, Arbitrum, Optimism, Polygon, Avalanche, Tron and Sui.

HYPE, currently the 11th-largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization, was not included in the latest withdrawal recovery plan, which raised concerns among investors.

As Binance proceeded to restore services following the recent security event, Hyperliquid community might be raising questions regarding the faith of their asset, considering none of it was lost, stolen or held by a hacker in any way.

On-chain investigator MLM (@mlmabc) covered the situation on X, writing: "Everything except HYPE (none of which was lost)."

Frustration over why HYPE withdrawals remain unavailable despite claims that the token was not among the assets stolen during the incident is covered by the official answer from Binance.

As stated by Binance's formal announcement, withdrawals are being restored progressively rather than all at once, in real-world scenarios. Unfortunately, Binance hasn't provided users with any dates regarding the opening of HYPE withdrawals.

The exchange officially answered, stating that each and every asset has to pass a security and readiness checks before withdrawals are opened. Binance also explained that the withdrawal demand surged crucially in the last few weeks.

Deposits, as always, remain available, even though withdrawals that have been opened previously might get delayed for some users due to demand, technical limitations and additional security checks by the team.

Considering the size of Hyperliquid on the market, its capitalization of $18.6 billion, a surge of volatility from a single exchange shouldn't disrupt its market performance, considering the rich presence of market makers and other liquidity sources.

It has been eight days since investors were able to withdraw their own holdings and it creates frustration among many users who went on X to express it. One user wrote: "It’s over once they do that’s why they haven’t."–an opinion quite common around assets whom largest market supply has been locked for more than a week.

Nick Woltemade only started one competitive match at Juventus in his first six weeks at the club after moving on loan from Newcastle United at the start of September.

Yet Big Nick bizarrely made the scapegoat for the poor start to the Juventus league season as they won only three of seven Serie A matches, none of which the Newcastle United loan star started…

The most recent of those games was last night.

West Ham have to reignite pursuit of Swansea City man in January to fix problem position: Opinion

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

West Ham could do with some reinforcements this winter to boost their squad ahead of the second-half of the season. WHUFC are currently positioned down in 8th place in the Championship table and they occupy the final position in the play-offs.

The Hammers were credited with an interest in Swansea City defender Ben Cabango in the summer as they eyed signings under former head coach Nuno Espirito Santo. However, nothing materialised in the end and he stayed put in South Wales beyond the last deadline in September.

The centre-back is a proven performer at second tier level and has made 289 appearances already in his career, 13 of which have come this season. He has also made 19 caps for Wales.

Cabango is one of Swansea City’s most prized assets and is their skipper. Snapping him up would be a big statement of intent by West Ham and a sign that they mean business now under Parker.

West Ham have conceded too many goals in this campaign so far. WHUFC have been too leaky at the back and need to tighten things up.

Cabango would help the Hammers strengthen their backline. Swansea City’s defensive record is up there with one of the best this term to date and his form has been a key reason behind that.

The Cardiff-born man would give Parker‘s side more competition and depth in his position. He could battle it out with the likes of Konstantinos Mavropanos and Max Kilman for a place in their starting XI.

He has been on the books at Swansea City for his whole career to date so a change of scene would do him good. Cabango rose up through their academy and was a regular at various different youth levels before breaking into the first-team back in 2019.

Swansea City are 5th and have aspirations of going up to the top flight themselves. Therefore, they are likely to have reservations about selling Cabango, especially to a league rival.

The Swans may find it hard to turn down big money for him though and everyone has their price. They have also lost three of their last four outings so are starting to fall away.

West Ham will only get stronger under Parker now and showed great fight against Cardiff City on Wednesday. They are back in action this weekend against Blackburn Rovers away.

Lost audio recordings of Louisiana Creole give us a rare chance to hear the past voices of early New Orleans

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Recordings from 1935 reveal how Louisiana Creole speakers switched languages, capturing speech patterns that written accounts often missed.

To understand how Louisiana Creole sounded in the 1800s, scholars have had to rely on writing. But some writers changed what they heard.

One researcher wrote in 1933 that he had “purified the dialect” in his transcriptions.

Recordings made two years later, now analyzed for the first time, capture something that those written accounts often missed: people moving between Creole and French as they spoke.

Lorenzo Dow Turner, remembered as the first African American linguist, recorded those voices on 13 aluminum discs, way back in 1935.

Oliver Mayeux, a linguist at the University of Cambridge’s Faculty of Modern and Medieval Languages and Linguistics, tracked down the digitized recordings and worked out who was speaking.

The discs contain the earliest known recordings of spoken Louisiana Creole and Louisiana French. Both languages now have dwindling numbers of elderly native speakers.

“Turner’s lost recordings contain far more linguistic diversity than the texts we relied upon until now,” said Mayeux.

“This is the language of the levees and cane fields. The language that drifted out from French Quarter saloons along with early jazz music at the turn of the last century.”

Louisiana Creole shares almost all its words with Louisiana French, but the two languages use different grammar.

Creole developed on southern Louisiana plantations, where enslaved people, mostly from West Africa, came into contact with settlers who spoke regional varieties of French.

“Africans with little common language were forced onto plantations where French was the language of administration. Everybody is learning approximations of French and then approximations of approximations. Quite quickly, a new language is born,” said Mayeux.

By Turner’s arrival in July 1935, younger people and city residents were already moving toward English. Mayeux, who has family ties in Louisiana, knows that history through his own family.

“By the middle of the 20th century you see the end of intergenerational transmission. My grandfather wanted his children to speak English,” explained Mayeux.

“This was typical of many Louisianans who saw their future as American rather than Creole or Cajun,” he added.

Many children, therefore, grew up without learning the language their parents spoke.

Turner was known for studying Gullah, a creole language with English roots spoken by Black communities in Georgia and South Carolina.

He visited Louisiana to compare the two languages’ sounds, especially how speakers’ voices rose and fell.

“From casual observation I find that the intonation of Creole resembles very much that of Gullah,” Turner wrote to a funding body after the trip.

His recording machine weighed about 100 pounds (45 kilograms). He had to drive it to places with electricity, which limited where he could work.

“Turner relied on a network of Black doctors and intellectuals in New Orleans and out in the country. The physicians found him Creole-speaking interviewees and let him record in their offices,” said Mayeux.

“These were trusted spaces for the participants during the era of Jim Crow segregation and, crucially, were wired for electricity.”

In Lafayette and Opelousas, doctors Julius Blackburn Matthews and August C. Terrence hosted the sessions. One participant, Marie Raymond, said she had never known a doctor until she was 69.

A woman also helped Turner conduct many interviews. She seemed more comfortable speaking French than he did, and Mayeux thinks she was probably Turner’s wife, Geneva Calcier Townes Turner.

Turner never analyzed the Louisiana Creole recordings, and his applications for funding to continue the comparison were rejected. The Guggenheim Foundation turned down one application and awarded a fellowship to his former student Zora Neale Hurston instead.

Mayeux suspects Turner simply set the discs aside. Indiana University’s Archives of Traditional Music later digitized them, but their labels offered little information and sometimes got it wrong.

One clue came from a disc that a Turner biography described as two people singing. It actually contained solo piano music by Jessie Covington Dent, a New Orleans pianist and civil rights activist.

Dent’s family papers at the Amistad Research Center led Mayeux to Turner’s papers. An address book there named the doctors who had hosted him.

Another disc listed “Mme Emma Bronssard,” which Mayeux recognized as a misspelling of Broussard. The woman said she was 69, had been widowed for 20 years, and had raised 12 children of her own plus four orphans.

Census and death records matched those details to Emma Benoit, born Emma Broussard.

Her great-granddaughter, Valencia Pradier-Linton, later shared a magazine interview in which Benoit’s son said his mother “didn’t know how to talk English at all.”

Turner recorded three Creole speakers in New Orleans, where people shifted to English earlier than in the countryside. The city’s variety of Creole likely has no speakers left.

Mayeux identified two speakers: George Doyle, born in 1867, and Odelia Edmond, born in 1862. Doyle had worked as a police officer before the city segregated its police department.

Some of their speech matched the old texts closely. Doyle’s word for policemen, “lapolis,” came from the French “la police,” but the French word for “the” had become a permanent part of the noun.

Edmond used mo for “my” when she said “my mother.” French would use a feminine form of the word instead.

Other details challenged what scholars thought they knew. The speakers used rounded vowels, like those in the French words “peu” and “plus,” which researchers had treated as a later borrowing.

Edmond also moved between old and newer forms of the same word. While explaining that she was three days old when her mother died, she used a long form of “have” known mainly from 19th-century writing.

Just 15 seconds later, when talking about being nine days old, she used the short form. Hearing both from the same speaker suggested that New Orleans Creole was more varied than the written record made it seem.

Outside New Orleans, speakers often began interviews in Louisiana French, the more formal language. Others in the room then asked them to speak Creole.

Emma Benoit started with a polite French greeting. In Mayeux’s translation, a woman interrupted with “no no no! Creole!” and then “no no that’s French; that’s not Creole anymore.”

Benoit replied in Creole, “I forget that,” and said she would talk about her troubles. A few minutes later, she returned to French and received another reminder.

Anastasie Dumas switched languages too, answering in French when Turner addressed her in formal, European-sounding French. When his co-interviewer asked in somewhat stiff Creole how to make gumbo, Dumas returned to Creole.

Most participants could not read or write and did not own a radio. Speaking into a visiting professor’s recording machine would have been an unusual, formal experience.

The people in the room and the occasion helped shape how they spoke. Mayeux concluded that French signaled politeness and respectability, while Creole emerged in more relaxed moments of joking and storytelling.

For most speakers today, moving between the two languages no longer carries the same degree of social meaning.

Each recording lasts about two to five minutes, and static makes some speech hard to follow. The New Orleans speakers also came through one circle of doctors, educators, and musicians, so their voices cannot represent the whole city.

Mayeux describes his reconstruction as partial and interpretive, shaped by his position as a white researcher listening nearly a century later. He hopes others will offer their own readings.

He also cannot yet explain why the city recordings differ from the old texts. The language may have changed, writers may have removed variation, or both may have happened.

The next step is comparison with recordings that Turner’s former student Raleigh Morgan Jr. made in the 1950s. Researchers can study those voices alongside the 1935 discs and the Creole spoken today.

For some young learners, Creole offers a way to reconnect with their family history. Many are grandchildren of native speakers whose parents never learned the language, Mayeux said.

“Young people are learning Creole to connect with the richness of their heritage, helping them discover a distinct identity in an increasingly homogenized world,” said Mayeux.

The study is published in the journal Language Documentation and Description.

Zillennials are ‘loud working ’: They’re spending nearly 5 hours a week on self-promotion—and driving colleagues to quit

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

So you’ve heard of the trend quiet quitting, and its sister, loud quitting—but now workers are doing the complete opposite: “loud working.” Instead of doing the bare minimum to get by or turning their anger on their employers, they’re pouring all their energy into making their work more visible.

Think of the colleague who posts every win on LinkedIn or pings the whole team on Slack the second a task is done.

New research from Tide, a finance platform, found that the average U.K. worker spends 131 hours a year—more than 17 working days or 2.8 hours a week—making their work visible. That includes singing their own praises on social media, posting updates on Slack or Teams, speaking up in meetings, and vocalizing their wins to anyone who will listen.

And the loudest of all are younger workers: In fact, nearly 70% of 25- to 34-year-olds are “loud working.” They’re spending about 5 hours every week self-promoting. And it’s drawing attention from management, clients and even angry coworkers.

Perhaps unsurprisingly, it’s not always the most competent worker who gets rewarded—it’s the one who makes the most noise about it.

According to the research, 77% of “loud workers” have had at least one career opportunity in the past year alone, including new customers or clients, pay rises and promotions. For 15% of respondents, getting vocal about their wins has even gotten them awards.

And all the new noise around their success is attracting the attention of other employers; In fact, 17% say they’ve not only been poached, but they’ve actually landed a new job because they’ve been more vocal about their work.

But worryingly, it’s not just “loud workers” who are at risk of being snapped up and leaving the business.

Not everyone’s cheering. More than one in four (27%) workers have considered leaving a job because of a loud working culture, or because colleagues are too vocal about their own achievements.

Zillennials are both most likely to be guilty of “loud working,” as well as the ones walking off.  They’re posting about their success at more than three times the rate of over-55s. Yet nearly half of 25 to 34-year-olds have considered quitting a job because of it—and one in five already have.

Some industries are more forgiving of office braggers than others: Workers in IT, telecoms, and finance are the most likely to have had enough of performative culture. Meanwhile, men are more likely than women to have already quit over it.

“Loudworking has mixed reactions in the workplace and it’s easy to understand that a colleague who turns every small win into an announcement might be off-putting,” said George Schmidt, Tide’s CEO for the U.K. and Europe. “But our research shows there’s a difference between being performative and backing yourself.”

He said that for small business owners, sharing their work, wins and expertise is often the most effective marketing they have. And the same goes for anyone who has left their employer to go solo or who is eyeing up the exit.

Ultimately, though, many workers want to be at a business that rewards people for actually doing the hard work—not just shouting the most about it.

Do you ‘loud work’—or have you quit over a colleague who does? Get in touch: orianna.royle@fortune.com

Orianna Rosa Royle is the Success associate editor at Fortune, overseeing careers, leadership, and company culture coverage. She was previously the senior reporter at Management Today, Britain's longest-running publication for CEOs.

Win at Everton would put Coventry on right side of history in survival fight

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

None of the 21 sides who had 12 points after 10 matches, over the past decade, were relegated from the Premier League

If Coventry win at Everton on Friday night they will take an enormous stride towards maintaining their Premier League status. That might seem a huge statement with 28 games remaining, but victory would put Frank Lampard’s side on the right side of recent history. For now, they stand on a precipice.

Coventry have taken nine points from nine league matches. Eight teams in the previous 10 seasons were in the same position – seven stayed up. The exception, Leicester in 2024-25, were newly promoted and have tumbled downwards ever since.

There were 21 clubs in the last decade with 12 points after 10 matches. None of them went down and eight finished in the top half. Lampard’s first return to a former club this season suddenly looks remarkably important. Even if a win cannot guarantee Coventry’s top-flight survival, they would occupy what has been a strong position historically.

Lampard has a similar managerial record to former teammate (and new West Ham manager) Scott Parker, a midfielder alongside whom he made 27 appearances for Chelsea. Excellent in the second tier - Lampard reached the playoffs at worst in each of his three Championship campaigns - he has never fully convinced in the Premier League. Given he took Chelsea to fourth place and the FA Cup final in 2019-20, perhaps that is unfair. Equally, they had finished third the year before and were champions in two of the four campaigns prior to that.

Though he kept Everton up in 2022, Lampard won only nine of his 38 league matches with the club. No Everton manager with at least as many games has a lower win percentage in all competitions, with that post followed by a caretaker spell back at Chelsea in which he won only once in 11 matches.

Regardless of Lampard’s ability, what can we reasonably expect from Coventry? Capology estimate the club have the lowest wage bill in the division, while Transfermarkt rate their squad as having the third-lowest market value. This challenging reality was reflected in pre-season predictions. Opta gave Coventry a better chance of staying up than their fellow promoted sides, Hull and Ipswich. The former have delivered the weakest performance of the three and are now deemed the most likely of the trio to go back down. They have faced all the current top three, in fairness, while Hull haven’t played any of them and Ipswich have only tackled one.

Coventry remain third-favourites for the drop, as they were in pre-season. It is just the teams below them that have changed, with Sunderland and Crystal Palace now propping up the forecast. With the possibility of Manchester City facing a points deduction, third bottom might be enough to remain in the Premier League.

That issue notwithstanding, Coventry’s underlying performance needs to improve. Not that anyone would have cared when they scored three times to beat Sunderland last weekend when only recording 0.69 in expected goals. Newcastle had the better of the chances when losing 4-1 at the Coventry Building Society Arena, too. Their match against Hull is the only one this season in which they were on the right side of the xG.

Coventry led the Championship for expected goal difference last season. Lampard’s Chelsea improved on this metric in 2019-20 from the season before and were only 0.6 behind champions Liverpool. The 48-year-old has shown he can lead teams to strong statistical performance, though Coventry are clearly far more of an underdog this season than Lampard’s sides were in his strongest campaigns.

Everton did not beat any of the top seven at the Hill Dickinson Stadium last season and lost 2-0 to Chelsea in their previous home league match. They have generally performed well against struggling sides, though. Their only home defeats to bottom-half sides last season were against Newcastle and Tottenham, two clubs who were in the Champions League.

Victory on Friday is a tall order for Coventry, but if Lampard can win at Everton for the first time in his managerial career, his side will be well set for the task of staying up.

Southampton have bounced back from their four point deduction penalty to start the campaign.

The Saints endured a tricky summer, with a lot of controversy surrounding their expulsion from the play-offs by the EFL.

But the Hampshire outfit are in the mix for a play-off place already after just the first couple months of the new campaign, despite starting with a handicap.

Tonda Eckert’s side have the same record after 14 games as the current league leaders Middlesbrough, and will have their sights set on a top two finish this year.

And the January transfer window could be an important period in their promotion battle, with incomings and outgoings expected, with Aaron Ramsdale’s departure being top of the list.

Here we look at the Southampton players that should follow the goalkeeper out of the door at St. Mary’s in the new year…

Welington joined Southampton in the 2025 January window from Sao Paulo, but has been unable to make much of an impact.

The left-back is comfortably behind Ryan Manning in the pecking order of Eckert’s side, with the Irishman proving to be a focal piece of the squad.

While it is important to have cover in the squad for the defender, Welington can’t be pleased with his lack of game time at the moment.

Whether it is a loan or a permanent move, he should be seeking an exit from St. Mary’s in the new year, as it is clear that he will never be more than a second choice at the Championship club.

Mads Roerslev’s first season with Southampton last year was interrupted by injury issues, which led to him only playing nine times in the league through the campaign.

But since recovering to full fitness, he has yet to earn a chance under Eckert, and it’s difficult to see him breaking into the squad.

With the likes of James Bree and Zach Abbott ahead of him in the pecking order, the January window presents a good opportunity for the full-back to seek a move elsewhere.

Otherwise, it’s difficult to see him earning any game time soon, and his departure could also open up valuable space in the squad for the second half of the campaign.

Southampton rejected offers for Samuel Edozie at the end of the summer transfer window, but he hasn’t been a big part of the squad since then.

The winger has started just two of the opening 14 games of the new campaign, making a further eight appearances from the bench.

Princewill Ehibhatiomhan spent the second half of last season on loan with Salford City, where he made 11 appearances in League Two.

Another loan move, or even a permanent departure, would make a lot of sense in the winter market.

QPR must be laughing at Charlton Athletic player situation

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Charlie Kelman's QPR career looked over after he was loaned out yet again to Leyton Orient in the summer of 2024, but an excellent campaign there gave the Hoops a decision to make.

The American frontman hadn't scored a goal for the R's in 26 appearances after arriving from Southend United three-and-a-half years earlier, spending time at Gillingham and Wigan Athletic in between his first spell at Orient during their 2022/23 League Two title-winning campaign.

Even despite the O's success that year, Kelman only scored seven in 43 fourth-tier outings, and after his time at Wigan only saw him score three in 14, the writing seemed to be on the wall with a fourth loan spell.

But Kelman blossomed into life at Brisbane Road, scoring 27 times in all competitions and helping Ritchie Wellens' side make the League One play-off final. Some questions were raised about whether QPR would keep him after that season, but they cashed in, and it's looking like a brilliant decision.

Charlton Athletic signed Kelman last summer for a guaranteed £3.5 million, with add-ons taking it to £5 million. For a player who QPR likely would have been happy cutting ties with for next to nothing the year before, it was a great bit of business.

At the time, it was a worthy price tag. The then-23-year-old had just won the League One Golden Boot and was the main surprise package in what was the surprise package team in the third tier that year at Leyton Orient.

Kelman played a part in every game for Ritchie Wellens during the 2024/25 campaign, playing 49 times in the league and play-offs, plus all rounds in the EFL and FA Cups and every game in their EFL Trophy campaign, too. So, he was durable and brilliant in front of goal.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

He was creating good chances consistently, too, not just being in the right place and benefiting from very clinical finishing, which can often skew opinion regarding a Golden Boot winner. Kelman's 21 league goals came from 51 shots on target and 18.60 xG.

The Championship beckoned for him, whether it be back at QPR or elsewhere. In the end, the Hoops decided to move forward with a different star striker from that League One campaign, Richard Kone, and offloaded Kelman to Charlton, who beat Orient in the play-off final.

QPR took a risk in allowing a striker who had just scored 27 goals the previous year to leave their club in the summer, almost chancing that this was a mere one-off after failing to score more than seven times in a single season before then.

And, ultimately, it's proven to be the right decision so far. Kelman hasn't lived up to the bill at Charlton just yet, despite some promising moments.

The American failed to score in his first 12 league outings for the Addicks, and after breaking that duck against Swansea City last November, he was sidelined for close to two months, something which didn't happen at all during his loan spell the previous year.

After coming back, he rarely started consecutive games, as Nathan Jones chopped and changed at the top of the pitch. His longest stint in the starting eleven was a four-game run in January.

He ended his debut campaign in Charlton colours with seven goals in 36 games in all competitions, playing around 2,800 minutes fewer than he did at Orient the year prior.

It hasn't been all bad for Kelman. When taking into account his minutes last season, he netted around a goal every three league games, and some of his strikes were brilliant, such as the one against Hull City in the penultimate match, which secured Charlton's Championship status.

Even this term, after coming back from a dislocated shoulder during pre-season, he scored a brilliant goal to give his side the lead against Norwich City. He just needs to find that small consistency to really prove himself as a solid second-tier striker.

How have you assessed Charlie Kelman's start to life at Charlton Athletic? Let us know in the comments.

Plainly put, before last season, Kelman wasn't a Championship-level frontman. There was always going to be a risk, from Charlton's perspective, in putting their faith in someone who had one good year at League One-level to step up and score his first second-tier goals at a similar rate.

So, it can be argued that the 25-year-old has improved over the short time he's spent at the Valley, but given how much the Addicks invested in him, you can't help but feel some disappointment at his output thus far.

It's not like QPR were shying away from giving a League One goalscorer a chance in the Championship last summer, though, and that's where the praise lies at Loftus Road.

The Hoops passed on re-integrating Kelman back into their own side and instead put their faith in Kone, who finished third in the goalscoring ranks the same season with Wycombe Wanderers, and Rumarn Burrell, who netted 11 that year at Burton Albion, to lead their line.

And so far, those two have shown more than the Charlton frontman has. Kone didn't explode into a 15-20 campaign, but he scored a respectable 10 Championship goals and remained available throughout the campaign.

Who are the most clutch players in the Championship that could whip in a last-minute set piece worldie?

Meanwhile, Burrell did burst onto the second-tier scene, netting 10 in 26 before going down with an injury which wiped out almost all of the second half of his season.

This year, they have six goals between them, and Kone's 13-minute hat-trick in their 5-0 drubbing of Swansea recently has many feeling that his second season at this level could see him push between 15 and 20 this time around.

QPR are definitely not missing Kelman at Loftus Road, but more crucially, the American isn't doing enough so far to make Julien Stephan's side feel any transfer envy.

New York Times In Exclusive Talks To Buy Letterboxd For Reported $350 Million

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The New York Times Company is in exclusive talks to acquire the beloved cinephile social media platform Letterboxd, Deadline has confirmed. The only other remaining bidder, A24, is out of the running.

The New York Times first reported that its parent is in pole position to buy the site from Canadian hedge fund Tiny, pegging the deal at $350 million.

The field was initially said to include Netflix, Sony, the former Paramount and others but most fell away as the price rose. Another suitor, a nonprofit called Intrinsic Entertainment, told Deadline tonight that it’s assembled a group of like-minded investors and was several weeks away from making a $300 million offer. Related Stories News Rider Strong Stopped Rating Films On Letterboxd "For The Sake Of My Career": "Not A Good Look" News As Letterboxd Draws Interest From The New York Times, A24 And More, One Of Its Bankers Calls It "A Phenomenon"

It isn’t not clear if the NYT bid includes the stakes of Letterboxd founders Matthew Buchanan and Karl von Randow, CEO and CTO, who each own 20% of the company after selling 60% to Tiny in 2023. Letterboxd said in a statement at the start of Tiny’s sale process that the duo, who are based in New Zealand, would be involved in any decision about Letterboxd’s future. Watch on Deadline

The report today said the NYT is considering leaving the duo at the helm. A24 had promised Letterboxd independence and to leave its business model unchanged.

Tiny had hired boutique investment bank LionTree to oversee the sale process. Reps from LionTree, Letterboxd, the New York Times and A24 didn’t immediately respond to a request for comment.

Speaking at an FT conference recently, Alex Michael, a LionTree senior managing director, raved about Letterboxd, calling it “a phenomenon” that attracts 30 million monthly users “with zero paid marketing.”

For Natural Volume, Wash Your Hair The French Girl Way

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We all know that French girls have that special je ne sais quoi (so much so that it’s inspired countless essays on this very site). When it comes to their hair, the style is nonchalant—and yet, somehow also skillfully cultivated. It’s hair that’s air-dries, falls just right, and retains that slightly tousled movement that gives no hint of effort or styling. So how exactly do Jeanne Damas, Brigitte Bardot, Jane Birkin, and more do it?

Hairstylist and French girl herself Daphné Narcy has an idea: It all starts how you wash your hair. “You may be damaging your hair without even realizing it while in the shower,” Narcy says. “When you’re hair is wet, it’s at its most fragile. But habits like washing hair too quickly, scrubbing too hard, or using water that’s too hot may be causing breakage and frizz.”

She offers up eight simple shower-time adjustments to turn your hair into a French girl dream.

The first—and by far the most common—mistake: People wash their hair instead of their scalp. “We rub the lengths against each other, which damages them, and we neglect the roots, where sebum, pollution, and residue accumulate,” says Narcy. Her philosophy can be summed up in one sentence: A healthy scalp is the foundation of healthy hair. It’s also what determines how light the roots feel once the hair is dry.

The heat from a scalding shower lifts the hair cuticle, which can be bad for shine. “The hair loses moisture, becomes more porous and duller, and the color fades faster,” Narcy says. Hot water hurts the scalp, too, making it drier and more irritated. “Water that’s too hot also softens the hair follicles, which can lead to hair loss.” Your new rule of thumb here is disarmingly simple: “If the water feels pleasant on your wrist, without feeling hot, it’s the right temperature for your hair.”

Use your fingertips in small circular motions, without ever scrubbing. “It’s meant to be an invigorating massage, not a scrub,” insists Narcy. It’s also a moment of pleasantness. When you enjoy it, washing your hair becomes a ritual rather than a chore.

If you’ve ever read the back of a shampoo bottle, you know the instructions: Lather, rinse, repeat. And Narcy says that isn’t just to get you to use more product; but instead the double-shampoo is key to keeping hair clean—though only use a walnut-sized amount each time.

“When you use too much shampoo, residue can remain on the scalp, weighing it down, potentially irritating it, and making your hair look dirty faster The result: you end up washing your hair more often, and you get caught in a vicious cycle.”

The lengths should only be lightly touched when it comes to shampooing. “Press your hands together and slide them down, using very light and gentle friction, all the way to the ends. While rinsing, the lather that runs down finishes cleansing the lengths.” Less friction means fewer tangles, less breakage, and mid-lengths that not only stay hydrated but also dry smooth.

"You Don't Want To Interrupt The Player's Experience" - Here's Why Zelda's Switch 2 Remake Cut Some Mario References

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Nintendo is known in recent times for dropping Star Fox (and even Pikmin) into the Super Mario universe, but with the Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake on the Switch 2, it decided to take a little more care.

In the castle courtyard, where Link meets with Zelda, you might have noticed how the character portraits for Mario and friends are not in the Switch 2 version of the game. If you are wondering about the thought process behind this, Zelda series producer Eiji Aonuma has explained how the team didn't want to break the immersion.

Here's what he had to say during the same chat with Game Informer:

Eiji Aonuma: "Certainly, at the time, when we put those [portraits] there, I don't think anybody thought it was too big a deal. Maybe there was a more carefree or comfortable approach to that sort of thing. But looking at it now, like if a player is coming through that area, and given the overall way that we're, you know, creating this world and giving the player that form of expression, if they look up and they think, "Why is Yoshi there?” We don't want that moment to interrupt their experience of the world in that way. So we're feeling a little bit differently about it this time."

If you recall, the 3DS game also had Skyward Sword art hidden in certain locations, and this is also gone from the Switch 2 remake. According to Aonuma, the same reasoning applies here - the team didn't want to "interrupt the player's experience by seeing something that's unrelated to what they're currently doing in the game".

Although these easter eggs are gone, certain references to the Mario series remain intact as they are part of the character designs:

"You will remember that there is one design element on Talon with Bowser on it, and that's part of the original design of the character. So we didn't change that. That stays as is."

What are your thoughts about this decision? Did you notice the change yourself? Let us know in the comments.

Liam is a news writer and reviewer on Nintendo Life. He's been writing about games for more than 15 years and is a lifelong fan of many iconic video game characters.

I wish they had kept the Mario reference in the courtyard window but made it only viewable if you stood in just the right place.

But at the same time, they changed a lot of things that were familiar to me about the original to reach kids whose parents might not even have been alive when the original came out.

There’s no reason they need to treat an all-time classic as a museum piece, because the original game will always be a masterpiece. Better to iterate upon it with fresh ideas and convenient tweaks than to declare any changes off-limits because of Nostalgia™.

The only major thing I would have done differently is to preserve the more poetic and succinct quality of the original English translation. I understand why they changed it for new audiences, but I still miss the original’s grand tone, and that’s one reason the 64 and 3DS versions will always have a place for me.

In general immersion is not something I have ever remotely cared about and I'd a lot rather just keep the random goofy references, but it's also not exactly a dealbreaker.

This seems sensible. It's nice of him to give a thorough and thoughtful explanation about it.

Also, I either forgot about it, or this is the first time I'm seeing the Bowser thing on his kerchief. Ha!

@Lizuka I don't care that they didn't add goofy references, and it may not have fit the tone of this as well, to his point. Yet I agree with you about immersion; it is not something I get very upset about in games, when something "breaks" it for me, like a notification or something. I know I'm playing a game. Movies might feel more noticeable to me. But I'm sure it's just a personal perspective.
