New Hampshire

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The penultimate state! Wow. Not only did it feel amazing to leave Vermont (finally!) but walking into such a highly anticipated state was surreal. Also Singe’s home state and homecoming! Even though I had never hiked in a lot of the prior states, I had at least visited a majority of them. But from the moment I stepped foot into New Hampshire, everything was brand new to me. I was trying to convey that New Hampshire and Maine would be new states for me but what I ended up saying was, “New state forever. Or…wait. How would a normal person say that?” To which Loon said, “How about ‘I’ve never been here before.'” lol.

Singe, Loon, and I walked across the Connecticut River into New Hampshire on a sweltering summer day in early August. Our first order of business? Jump right into the river. Before strolling into Hanover, right after the bridge, you can take a left, walk to the Dartmouth dock, ignore all the signs about it being for students and staff only, and jump right in. It felt AMAZING. I cannot emphasize enough how hot the sun was and how exposed the concrete bridge is. The river was full of weird cold pockets that we enjoyed to the max. Once we had cooled down, we finally walked into Hanover! I was super excited because I’m a little nerd who loves college campuses and Hanover being both a college town and a hiker town had always stuck with me.

Hanover was so cute and Dartmouth was so beautiful. Shocking. We found the Dartmouth Outdoors Club and left our packs in their care. Then we walked around Hanover and cashed in on some awesome hike friendly deals! We got free pizza for hikers and were completely entranced by the Housecleaning Games on ESPN 8: The Ocho. My favorite part? A poster that said “Ouchtown: Population YOU.” Incredible. We got handmade ice cream at Red Kite Candy which remains one of my most memorable ice cream stops on trail. I had Strawberry Balsamic. Thank you for asking. We grabbed our packs and walked around campus a little before getting picked up to stay with one of Singe’s good friends in town. It was so exciting to get a look into Singe’s life and meet the characters and friends she’s been talking about all trail!

We had an awesome night getting to know one of Singe’s oldest friends and having a homemade pot roast. YUM. Also this night, I got an email that I got off the waitlist for a marathon the first weekend of October. In Connecticut I started toying with the idea of running a marathon after trail just to see what would happen. When I received this email offering me a spot, I went for it. Turns out, this is the same marathon that Loon’s friend, who we have seen multiple times on trail, was training for! Done and done. The rest of the hike, this marathon was a sort of bit. I would forget I signed up and someone would mention it and I’d be like “oh RIGHT! The marathon! Yes, of course.” What a bit.

The next day, we were able to leave most of our things at Singe’s friend’s house because we were staying with another Singe friend connection the next night! We walked over Velvet Rocks, right off the campus of Dartmouth, and to our pick up spot near the Ice Cream Man’s house where I discovered my new favorite Key Lime popsicle. We got to hang out with the cutest dog’s at Singe’s friend’s parents’ house and enjoy their company and a delicious home-cooked meal.

We were back to it with full packs this time. We were coming up close to Singe’s house and sections of trail she’s done several times. The plan was for Loon and I to spend the night out on trail but Singe was going to get picked up by her partner and stay at her house. We would join them the next day. It feels weird to be apart from Singe for even a night but I loved having a night with just Loon. We plotted to stay at Hexacuba Shelter because it seems weird and cool. Like I’ve said many times before, you have to look for reasons to get excited for the day. Hexacuba is a shelter in a hexagon shape with a pentagon shaped privy. Obviously, we had to see that. It turned out to be just the two of us at the shelter that night so we threw up our tents inside to have some protection from the bugs. We had the sweetest night camping and laughing together.

We climbed up and over Smarts Mountain and onto the fire tower the day before Hexacuba and Cube Mountain the morning after Hexacuba. I loved both! On top of Cube Mountain, we found a rude comment someone had left on a post of mine that had been shared and honestly had a field day with it. This person’s mean comment (and others they would continue to leave on my posts later) became one of our tramily’s favorite inside jokes. We took it well and not seriously but a little reminder: If you don’t have anything nice to say, don’t say it! This can really hurt people! Especially online!

Anywho! After clearing these two mountains, covering more ground, and taking a perfect midday lake swim, Singe picked us up and we went back to her house! It was so fun to see where our trail bestie lived after talking about it for so many miles. Spoiler: It’s beautiful. We took a zero with her and got to see some of New Hampshire that’s off trail too. I didn’t know I was going to fall in love with New Hampshire so quickly! There was a lot of anticipation because as soon as we left the comfiness of Singe’s house, we would be entering the Whites. But of course we were able to scheme a slack-pack and were able to not bring full packs over Mt Moosilauke by dropping our things off at The Notch Hostel a day early.

I was quite nervous and intimidated of the Whites before we started. But step by step, we ascended the mountain. I actually flew up to the top pretty fast. It’s difficult, but I think I had it built up so much in my mind that it was better than I expected. I did fall in a little tiny creek on the way up (lol) but it didn’t get me down. And when I broke the treeline? I was so speechless and energized that nothing really mattered. I kept squealing to myself out of excitement. The terrain is gorgeous and we had a beautiful day. I loved being able to see the path ahead of me and the progress I was making pushing to the summit. There were a ton of people hiking at the top but I was in my own little world taking it all in…until I heard a group to my left singing out “You’re a bear and I’m a girlllll.” AKA the Song of the Summer AKA the song I sang to a bear when I had a close encounter back in New York. I looked over and our trail cousins, The Binguses were sitting together serenading me. What a welcome to the top! This was the first of several mountain top reunions that kept happening in the Whites. So much fun. I went over and had lunch with them and waited for Singe and Loon to summit. We hadn’t seen the Binguses since we were all in New York City together in early July! We also got to meet a new Bingus they’d picked up along the way in Vermont.

We all hung out together at the top. Apparently some kind of eclipse was happening but not a full eclipse so we didn’t end up sticking around to see it from the top since the weather looked like it might be turning too. We all bunched up together for a picture on the summit that a day hiker took. It looked like she was taking several. She said to us, “How many do you want?” So we figured we’d have good options. But when she returned the phone, we had exactly three pictures. lol. Us Golden Girls and the Binguses all tackled the steep and endless descent together. We stopped a few times to look at the pretty waterfalls that follow the trail. It was so fun to be back with them again for this hike. We all got to the parking lot and waited for the shuttle to the Notch. A few of the Binguses weren’t staying at the hostel so they found a hitch into town.

We got picked up to head to the Notch by the owner, Serena, who also happens to be Singe’s good friend and climbing partner in normal life! She was awesome! Please stay at the Notch! They are so great and have their systems figured out so smoothly. Serena told the 6 of us in the shuttle (3 Golden Girls + 3 of the Binguses) that a local outdoor apparel company, Burgeon, wanted to invite 6 hikers for a pan pasta dinner tonight. So of course we were like, “we’ll we’re 6 hikers…” And voila! We checked in, showered, and an hour later we were on our way to have one of the best meals of my life. Three workers at Burgeon ate with us in their on-site stock room. They were cool and fun and we had a great time. This was one of the coolest and most exciting trail magics we had. Loon asked if they needed a Colorist to work for them since I work in fashion as a colorist! Thanks for trying to get me a job, Loon!

After the dinner, Singe’s partner offered to pick us up and take us to Super Secret Ice Cream. An ice cream shop in Bethlehem, NH that Singe had been talking up since probably day 2 of me knowing her. As an ice cream connoisseur and an AT Dairy-Blazer, I couldn’t wait. We left the boy Binguses behind and Loon, Singe, Outlaw, and I got ice cream. I am absolutely obsessed with this place. The branding, the colors, the unique small-batch flavors, the ice cream flights, the posters–all of it is perfect. New favorite ice cream. I think it’s extremely tragic that I live so far away from it and also probably for the best. If you’re even remotely close though, take the detour! Best ice cream I’ve had on trail and also in years hands down.

All of this together made for such a magical day and one of the standouts from trail. Tackling a hard and anticipated mountain but feeling strong? That view?! Reuniting with our friends unexpectedly! Surprise trail magic pasta dinner?! AND SUPER SECRET ICE CREAM?! What a way to kick off the Whites.

The next day we slackpacked the Kinsmens with The Notch. They have a deal that if you stay two nights, the slackpack is included. Yes please! The Kinsmens were some of the most difficult parts of the Whites. It wasn’t my worst day there, but quite a challenge. The final ascent up is basically bouldering/rock scrambling. I was so glad to not have my full pack weight, but I did feel extremely rushed trying to catch the shuttle back to the Notch. The options were a 4pm or 7pm. We got a tiny bit of a later start that ate into our hiking time. Thus, I ended my hike around 4:10pm while feeling extremely rushed and stressed the last part of the day. The trail was super rocky and frustrating. We missed the shuttle but since Singe did know the staff well, they came and picked us up. Thank you! When we got back to the hostel, someone had decided to make dinner for everyone as trail magic. What a godsend. Two covered dinners in a row. I was so incredibly exhausted after the Kinsmens that I probably would have cried if I had to figure out my own dinner. Moosilauke felt like a warm up but the Kinsmens felt like an ass-kicking. I was worried more of that was to come but Singe told me she thinks the Kinsmens are some of the hardest 4kers in the Whites and it shouldn’t feel worse than that which really helped. Tomorrow, we’d be back to full packs and really getting into it!

There is a challenge in New Hampshire hiking called the New Hampshire 48. This refers to a group of 48 prominent peaks all above 4k. The AT goes right over a few of them, and grazes along the side of a few others. Some people choose to tag the summits and take the detours to hit as many summits as they can. In hiking this pursuit of summits is called “Peak Bagging.” While we went through the Whites, Loon was all in on peak bagging as many of the 48 as she could. She was a rockstar! Singe and I supported her and encouraged her but did not have the same drive. I was too worried about my ankles on all the rocky ups and downs that I didn’t want to risk anything by doing extras. Because of this, Singe and I sometimes had slightly different days than Loon. Like for example, when Loon walked an extra like 7 miles to get 4 extra peaks in a day and Singe and I walked 5 extra miles the next day to be able to go get ice cream. It’s important to know where your priorities are.

Still, on the AT, I got my fair share of the 48 done. The trail follows a section of the Whites called the Pemigewasset Loop (or Pemi Loop) for about 10 miles after the Kinsmens. Most notably, it follows along Franconia Ridge which is an exposed ridge walk that goes over multiple peaks. You can see the circle of the Pemi Loop through the mountains while up on the ridge. I absolutely loved Franconia Ridge. It’s like nothing else on the trail. It most closely reminded my of a hike I had done in the Dolomites! And yet it’s here! Just sitting up in New Hampshire! Wow! Franconia is known for being beautiful but also extreme due to the exposure. Like everything in the Whites, it’s quite steep (like straight up rock ladders for miles) to get up to the top and then open to strong winds and weather with nowhere to hide. We were lucky to have gorgeous weather throughout the Whites. While we did have some wind (including being pushed over by the wind a few times), it was nothing unmanageable or unpleasant on Franconia. Still, the exposure is tough. There was even a Search and Rescue on this section the same day we were up there. But we got through it just fine along with my new can of Honey Mustard Pringles that we became obsessed with and didn’t last more than 6 miles. We were in such awe the whole time. When I first got to the top of the ridge, I ran into McMurphy! Another mountain top reunion! Loon started a little further back to get more peaks, so we also reunited with her on a summit!

Towards the end of the ridge, the novelty had worn off a little and we were ready to get out of the wind and set up camp for the night. We went down and then up and over Mount Garfield where I had another summit reunion! I ran into Sniper, a woman I had met in Georgia and North Carolina. She hiked the AT years ago and is attempting a Yo-yo of the full trail this year! This means she hiked from Georgia to Maine and will turn around and hike all the way back down to Georgia. Incredible!! It was so cool to run into her and see her still crushing it! After leaving her and wishing her the best on the rest of the journey, I made my way down to the Garfield Campsite where of course we ran into several other friends!

The Whites are known for their Hut-to-Hut system where you can stay in little lodges along the way. This is awesome for a stand alone trip, but not really an option for thru-hikers. They are quite expensive (especially multiple nights in a row) and require reservations far in advance. That would be nearly impossibly to schedule on a thru-hiker’s flexible and ever-changing timeline. Stealth camping is also not allowed/highly discouraged in the Whites due to the extreme weather and the delicate nature of the alpine plants. There are designated campgrounds throughout the Whites that each have caretakers. They are all first come first serve, but the caretakers are great at finding spots for everyone so we were never too stressed about it. At this first campsite, we registered for a thru-hiker pass with the caretaker that allows us to camp at all the campgrounds for a discounted price and also get free pastries and soup when we stop into the huts! Wahoo! We opted to stay in the shelter the first night because of the wind.

The next day we started off our hike by basically having to walk straight down a sort of rocky waterfall. The Whites are on another level. We made it to the Galehead Hut for lunch where we cashed in our free soup pass. Then we continued up South Twin Mountain. For some reason, when we summited, I could not get “Breathe” by Faith Hill out of my head and I continued to sing it as my anthem the rest of trail. South Twin was a long push up, but the descent felt flat compared to the climb. What a treat! We went to a campsite a little off the path since there weren’t too many options otherwise. You’d either have to do a really short day or a really long one. Given the strenuous terrain, we opted for a short day. Singe and I got to the campsite around 3pm while Loon went off to go bag three more peaks! It felt really nice to have time to relax and recover. I don’t think I got to camp before 4pm since North Carolina. We stayed in the shelter this night too and watched a beautiful sunrise from the comfort of our sleeping bags the next morning.

Our next day in the Whites was a bit of a Choose Your Own Adventure. This was one of the only nights on trail (the only night on trail?) that Singe, Loon, and I were all apart. I had a field day telling people we were all apart because we were fighting. Nobody believed it for a second. We had a bit of an easier day planned passing by Zealand Hut and aiming for Ethan Pond Shelter. But Singe’s friend told us she could meet us at the trailhead for Zealand Hut and take us to lunch in Bethlehem and then return us to trail. Loon decided to pass and stick with the original plan since she was already doing so many extra miles and peaks but all Singe had to tell me was we could get Super Secret Ice Cream again and I was in. Singe left a little before me in the morning but we met up at Zealand Hut. It was super special to be there with her because it was the first place she decided she ever wanted to Thru-Hike back in college. <3 We took a side trail that was super flat about 2.5 miles to a parking lot. I timed myself on how long it took from Zealand to the lot because I had the idea that I might try to return later for Work-For-Stay if the timing worked out. We had a great lunch and ice cream of course and were returned to the trailhead. It felt like we snuck off campus in the middle of the day with our cool older sister. We were cutting it close but I wanted to try for the work for stay at Zealand since it’s a unique thru-hiker experience and I didn’t know when else the timing would work out to try. I ZOOMED through the 2.5 miles back up to Zealand in record time and ta-da! I was granted one of the three work for stay spots that night! Singe ended up camping solo sort of in between Loon at Ethan Pond and myself at the hut.

Two more hikers I knew rolled in and we hung around the pretty waterfall waiting for our duties to begin. We got leftovers from the dinner that night and then were assigned the tasks of sweeping, wiping down tables, and reorganizing the freezer for the hut croo. In exchange, we got the dinner leftovers and were able to sleep in the dining room which was much appreciated since it rained that night. We had to get up super early so the staff could make breakfast for the paying guests. We hung around outside until breakfast leftovers were set out and then we descended on those. From there we made our way to the AMC Highland Center where Loon, Singe, and I met back up for a zero. I had a job interview over the phone the next day and I was touched by Singe and Loon’s efforts to make sure we were somewhere I could take the call with reliable service. We were excited for a rest too since the Whites are so demanding even when dropping the number of miles you’re doing each day. The Highland Center was also super neat because we got to run into our friend, Mosey, who we met during the first half of trail and was an original Bingus! The Binguses also trickled in over the next day and we got to catch up with them as well!

The interview went well (though did not ultimately land me a job…but that’s story for Maine) and I was feeling excited. There was no laundry so we did our best to freshen our clothes up in the bunkhouse sink. We enjoyed taking over the on-site library to do our nails and watch TV on our phones together. It was nice to have a little break before the Presidential Range or “The Prezies.” We actually lucked out by taking a zero for my interview because it let some bad weather pass and gave us a perfect weather window later!

After our time at the Highland Center and mapping out our next stretch, we made our way back up into the range. We made our way to the Mizpah Hut and Nauman Campground. Mizpah was one of my favorite huts. There was something more remote, cozy, and mellow about it more than the others I had seen. The Croo was also very friendly. We checked on a work-for-stay just to see but they didn’t have any so we camped at the Nauman Campground next door. Singe, Loon, and I got an entire tenting platform to ourselves! We didn’t know it then, but this would be the last night the three of us would camp together without anyone else. We had a magical time. Somehow, it wasn’t too cold so we decided to cowboy camp on our platform under the clear sky. We watched a beautiful sunset and had such a fun and special night. We were laughing so much. This night was such a highlight on trail. We slept under the stars and I remember waking up at different times during the night and seeing how the milky way was moving across the sky. What an experience.

The next day we got breakfast leftovers at Mizpah Hut with another female hiker who was traveling with her partner. I think she enjoyed having some girl time with us and we loved it too! We made our way from the hut up to the Mount Pierce summit (President Pierce not Pierce Brosnan apparently) and were officially in the Prezies! The first summit was not so bad but we were hyped! We could see the others stretching before us, most of which we’d tackle that day! We took a moment at the summit to talk about how excited we were. Before we continued on, Singe said, “Does anyone have words they’d like to share about Pierce before we continue?” which made me laugh all day thinking about it.

Mount Eisenhower was next and it was so beautiful! We had a gorgeous day and could see Mount Washington’s summit for the first time! Though it was quite windy, Eisenhower was one of my favorite mountains in the Prezies because the 360 views are so stunning! What a view of Washington! After a snack break and some photos, we kept it moving before getting too cold. We went up and over Little Monroe and Mount Monroe where we got a look at Lakes of the Clouds Hut where we would be staying. We decided to go ahead and book a full guest spot for Lakes of the Clouds because we wanted to experience the hut culture as guests and because it’s tricky to find a place to camp if you show up to this hut and there’s no room for thru hikers. Everything is exposed and there’s no camping around. By this point, you’re at the base of Mount Washington and frankly, out of options. We felt like we had such a magical day hopping from summit to summit and squealing about the views and the weather. Also, mileage wise, Lakes of the Clouds is not so far from Nauman Campground so we made it to the hut in really good time. Such good time, in fact, that we decided to go ahead and summit Mount Washington that afternoon! Originally we were going to wait until the next day but when you get good weather and you have the time…don’t waste that opportunity. We decided to go up the 1.5 trail to the summit with slackpacks and take a trail around the next day.

Washington was….fine. Loon, Singe, and I pressed play on the Hamilton soundtrack at the same time to get us up the mountain. It’s so strange to make your way up this infamous beast of a mountain only to be met with a bunch of ill prepared tourists who drove or took the Cog Railroad up. It was crowded and touristy, but hey, at least we weren’t hit with 100mph winds! We didn’t stay terribly long before making our way back to the hut. We knew 3 of the Binguses were making their way to the hut tonight too and we were excited to see them. Our friend, Mosey, was also able to work on-site at Lakes of the Clouds that day and spend the night too! The Binguses had to come from further so they had more of an exhausting day but and were wiped. Still, it was great to see them and spend some time with them after dinner when everyone went outside to take in one of our best sunsets of trails. I heard Brewski say, “We got to do stars tonight” about remembering to stargaze and it cracked me up enough that that phrase became a part of our lexicon the rest of trail.

While the first half of the Prezies were a dream, the second half were a bit of a nightmare. I did not enjoy day two of the traverse. The landscape was completely exposed all day and so rocky and unstable. It felt endless and frustrating. Maybe everything we’d been doing physically caught up to me and the novelty wore off? Either way, yikes. We made our way around Washington towards Jefferson where I did decide to tag the summit even though it was technically off trail. I got to the top and felt very regretful that I wasted the effort. I did not go up to tag Mount Adams. We made our way to Madison Hut where we ran into half of the Binguses again and commiserated about the day with them. I remember us telling Brewski “Can you please fake a medical emergency so we can all get airlifted out? You’re still on your parents’ insurance so it has to be you.”

We were trying to explore other options or side trails for getting down. We were going to meet Singe’s parents this night and stay with them in an airbnb and zero the next day but the idea of having a bed to sleep in felt too far away while we were traversing around. We wanted very badly to not have to go up and over Mount Madison and down to Pinkham Notch but there was really no other route. Eventually, we just had to go. The ascent up Madison really wasn’t so awful but the trail down? If you can even call it a trail? Was the absolute worst part of the entire AT for me. Everything was so exposed and unstable and endless and I felt like I would be stuck on the mountain forever. The progress was glacial and the steps were slow. I was without a doubt angry hiking. I remember looking for any sliver of hope and saying, “It’s only .9 left to the treeline!” and Loon, the beautiful and literal hiker she is, said, “And then it gets even steeper!” To which I yelled something like, “SHUT UP WHY WOULD YOU SAY THAT TO ME?!” We weren’t really angry at each other and it’s a moment we laughed about a lot throughout the rest of trail. I seriously think the descent down Madison is extremely dangerous and I cannot believe it isn’t talked about more. ANYWAYS. We did get down to the treeline and for me and my knees, it felt at least a little better walking on dirt and having trees to cling to if it was too steep.

We made it down to the Osgood Campsite where a random man appeared out of nowhere and tried to give me unsolicited advice. I was not having it. We kept joking that throughout this hike our faith in humanity has gone up but our tolerance of men has gone down. Once Singe and Loon made it down too, we took the original AT instead of the detour so that we could make it to Pinkham Notch for Singe’s parents to pick us up. What looked flat on the map ended up being flat with some random sharp ups and downs. Normally this would be okay, but when we’re already feeling so worn out, this was maddening. One of Singe’s friends had originally planned to meet us SOBO during the hike but wasn’t able to make it all the way up so she left us a bag of trail magic which we found towards the end of the day. It was a really sweet mental boost. We were about .2 from the Mount Washington Auto Road and 2 miles from Pinkham Notch. It was almost 7pm and we knew we were losing daylight quickly. Loon ran ahead to see if we could hitch down to the auto road entrance with the last of the cars leaving Mount Washington. The road up closes at 6pm but we hoped we could catch a hitch from some stragglers on the way out. We saw exactly two cars. One was a minivan that slowed down to tell us they didn’t have room even though we could clearly see they did. I started to cry. The next was a truck. I continued to pathetically cry while we asked for a ride to hopefully sway the decision. Unfortunately, it was a Washington worker who wasn’t allowed to give a ride in the work vehicle. We completely understood and that was disappointing but fine with us. The real kick in the pants was actually when he said, “The quickest thing for you to do will be to take the AT 2 miles down to Pinkham Notch.” WE KNOW SIR! My knees and feet were beat to crap from the day and I knew I didn’t have two more trail miles in the dark in me. We decided to walk down the auto road to where Singe’s dad could pick us up.

Loon, Singe, and I stayed in an airbnb with Singe’s parents near North Conway. We had a zero where we got our errands done and were also able to do a big fat nothing. Thank you Mr. & Mrs. Singe for your generosity once again!! The Binguses also took a zero at Mosey’s so we were all aligned for the next stretch! On Sunday, we set back out for the stretch between The Wildcats (where we listened to High School Musical) and the Carters (where I listened to Beyonce). We stayed overnight at Imp Shelter. The weather was drizzly but still could have been worse and was better than we expected. This is quite a steep section to do in the wet. Singe and I got to the shelter first so we watched Hacks on her phone before others showed up. I did spill an entire new bag of mini M&Ms but luckily my spirits were restored enough to think this was funny and not too tragic.

The next day, us Golden Girls and half of the Binguses hiked from Imp Shelter over Mount Moriah and down to the 1900 mile marker! We are right on the heels of Maine! We were greeted by The Ice Cream Man who gave us Klondike bars from his camper. We met some cool SOBOs and took pictures at the final 1900s mile marker before getting picked up by a friend I had made hiking out in the Dolomites last year. We met on a practice hike I did in Italy (I used practicing for the AT as an excuse to hike in really cool places and I recommend you do the same). I actually twisted my ankle (shocking) and he helped wrap it up since he is a SAR volunteer in the Whites! We got to talking while staying at some of the same huts and he told me to reach out when I came through on the AT and he and his wife would host me and whomever! It felt surreal to know this other milestone had been met. I was getting to see the New Hampshire connection I had before hiking.

My friend and his wife were so lovely and generous to us. We showered and enjoyed their company. My friend told us stories of his best SARs. We made taco bowls for them in exchange for the homestay and Strider made an apple pie from scratch!! The next day, we stayed talking over breakfast way longer than we planned because they were just so fun. My friend ended up letting me drive his car back to trail and leave a pin for him to pick it up later. Like I said, they were extremely generous to us. It felt a little funny to drive my trail friends in his car. We made it to the trail head and continued on! We had a long day pushing to the first shelter in Maine! We went up and over Mt Success which is the last mountain in New Hampshire. At the top, we learned the news that Dolly Parton had died. RIP. We also found one of our favorite FarOut comments on trail. If you scroll to the very beginning of the Mt Success comments it says “take that Dad!” Nice.

Soon we crossed into Maine! It immediately became about 10x more extreme just like everyone had promised. But also 10x more breathtaking. We saw a double rainbow while crossing over the Goose Eye mountains which I like to think was for Dolly. I thought maybe I’d feel more emotional crossing into Maine. Certainly it’s a big moment, but there’s still a lot of work to be done. Still, I did it. At the very least, my legs and feet have taken me from Georgia to Maine.

'Breaking Bad' Dean Norris' Dire Financial Situation Detailed in Restaurant’s Bankruptcy

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Actor Dean Norris and his estranged wife’s compensation and other financial details were revealed in their restaurant’s bankruptcy ... TMZ has learned.

Swing Inn Cafe, the restaurant Dean and his estranged wife, Bridget Norris, purchased in 2022, filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy last month.

Per the court docs, the restaurant, located in Temecula, California, had $147K in assets but owed over $1.8 million in liabilities.

Dean and Bridget said Swing Inn Cafe pulled in annual gross revenue of around 6.8 million in 2024, $5.9 million in 2025, and $4.1 million so far in 2026. The bankruptcy reportedly came after revenue dropped following social media attention fading after 2025.

Other debts listed in the bankruptcy docs include approximately $293K owed to Sysco -- which is a company that provides food to restaurants -- plus around $16K owed to a California bakery, and various other unpaid amounts.

In a recent filing, Bridget asked the court to authorize the company to fund payroll or the business will be ruined. She fears employees will quit if they aren’t paid … and she says, if that happens, the restaurant won't be able to operate, and revenues will plummet even further.

As TMZ first reported, Dean and Bridget aren’t only dealing with the bankruptcy ... but also have recently split up. Bridget filed for divorce in 2025 after over 23 years of marriage and five kids together. Bridget asked the court for spousal support.

Simkhai Pre-Fall 2027 Menswear

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As a designer mainly based in Los Angeles, Jonathan Simkhai can’t help but be influenced by the California coast: It’s in his fashion DNA. But if you think that means board shorts and flip-flops, think again. His pre-fall menswear fuses a free-spirited West Coast sensibility with a little more refinement—alternating between structure and ease. “We started with our great wardrobe heroes, and really played with color and fit,” he said.

Simkhai has been steadily building his menswear business these past few seasons, and his new assortment continued its focus on easy sportswear. Classic red polo sweaters were given sailor collars, and paired with his sleek pleated “Elvis” trousers. His blue-and-white striped polos were updated in cashmere-cotton blends that were soft to the touch. More technical windbreakers, a trending piece, were offered in subtle black and white or punchy red—the perfect thing to wear if you own a sailboat, and want to spend a day at sea. (One can dream.)

If things started to feel too coastal, Simkhai roughed them up with a bit of edge. The standout outerwear was a black funnel-neck leather jacket, boxy and with a sheen finish. Simkhai also offered luxurious suede button-up shirts that are great for those not-too-hot, not-too-cold days. His take on a classic denim jacket, meanwhile, was cropped and given a more nipped waistline.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/ardent-health-boosts-2026-guidance-volume-gains-expense-cuts" hreflang="en">Ardent Health boosts 2026 guidance on volume gains, expense cuts</a>

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
Ardent Health boosts 2026 guidance on volume gains, expense cuts" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Ardent Health bumped up its 2026 earnings guidance as expense and efficiency improvements met solid “top of the funnel” demand for care during the third quarter, the for-profit shared this week.

The performance comes after a soft second quarter, with executives on Thursday’s earnings call noting that pain points like surgical volumes improved sequentially while broader challenges like Affordable Care Act marketplace dropoffs remain within projections.

Ardent’s shares were trading 20% above open as of Thursday afternoon in spite of Wall Street’s economy-wide selloff.

Leaders said that the company’s Project IMPACT (Improve Margins, Performance, Agility and Care Transformation) initiative is on track to hit “at least” its expected $70 million benefit for 2026, and highlighted a 4% year-over-year reduction in the quarter’s salaries, wages and benefits (SWB) expenses during the quarter driven in part by contract labor expense reduction.

Meanwhile, Ardent is reviewing its portfolio to determine which assets are a long-term strategic fit—leading to decisions like September’s divestiture from a joint venture with the University of Kansas Health System—and plans to deploy its capital toward organic growth in its mid-sized urban markets while maintaining a “disciplined approach” to acquisition or joint venture opportunities that prioritizes above-average potential market growth, CEO Dave Caspar told analysts Thursday.

“When we spoke with you last quarter, I outlined a clear set of priorities: deliver more consistent financial results, grow EBITDA, deploy capital effectively and execute against our commitments in a way that creates long-term shareholder value,” Caspar said during the earnings call. “… Taken together, our third-quarter performance and recent strategic portfolio optimization demonstrate meaningful progress against the priorities I outlined.”

For the third quarter, Ardent reported $1.62 billion in total revenue (3.1% year-over-year growth), $45 million of net income and $130 million in adjusted EBITDA.

The health system's admissions grew by 0.1% and its adjusted admissions by 2.9%, year-over-year improvements over the second quarter, with net patient service revenue per adjusted admission increasing 0.7% since Q3 2025. Inpatient surgeries dropped by 5% and outpatient surgeries by 0.6% compared to the prior-year period, with Chief Financial Officer Alfred Lumsdaine noting that about half of those drops were tied to the health system's "intentional rationalization of certain lower-margin service lines in targeted locations."

Executives said they were happy with the volumes, noting that a 7% drop in exchange admissions and corresponding self-pay increase, similar to Q2’s 8%, was “manageable and largely contemplated in our guidance.” They particularly pointed to strong showings at the top of their “demand funnel,” or areas like urgent care or physician visits that can lead to downstream utilization. Part of the company’s ongoing improvement efforts are focused on boosting access to these offerings while reducing “friction as we move patients through the journey” to capture more care.

“For us, there's a lot of we'll call it embedded growth or backed-up growth that we just need to unlock by reducing friction and ensuring that we're making the process of converting the top of the funnel to the bottom of the funnel easier,” Lumsdaine said.

On the expense side, executives said Project IMPACT helped pull SWB spending down to 39.9% of revenue during the quarter, a 300-basis-point improvement from last year, and slash contract labor from 3.5% of total SWB to 2%. Professional fee expense growth also declined from Q1 2026’s 12.9% to Q3’s 7.4%, with that slowdown expected to slow further in Q4. Supply growth was “a modest 2.6%,” per Lumsdaine.

The performance and expected efficiency gains led Ardent to adjust its full-year 2026 guidance. While revenues are still anticipated to land on “the lower end of our $6.4 billion to $6.7 billion range,” the target for adjusted EBIDTA rose 1.5% at the midpoint.

On expectations for 2027, they declined to outline any specific number targets but noted well-recognized industry headwinds like Medicaid work requirements, marketplace plan attrition, as well as tailwinds like the Project IMPACT performance improvements and the recently finalized outpatient payment rule.

“I'm extremely proud of the progress our team has made this year, despite very fluid industry and macroeconomic dynamics,” Lumsdaine said. “We continue to strengthen our operating model, and we remain focused on building on our performance, generating strong cash flow and creating long-term shareholder value by aligning our people, capital and assets with the highest value opportunities.”

Ardent Health’s operations include 30 acute care hospitals and over 280 care sites, several of which are run as part of a joint venture structure with other health system partners. In 2025, the company increased its revenues by 6% to $6.3 billion and grew its adjusted EBITDA to $545 million.

→ Baca artikel asli

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/cigna-beats-street-14b-profit-737b-revenue-q3" hreflang="en">Cigna offers investors a look at its new Lead to One strategy</a>

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
Cigna offers investors a look at its new Lead to One strategy" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Cigna offered investors additional color on its new healthcare transformation strategy on Thursday morning.

First unveiled at the company's investor day on Sept. 30, the Lead to One initiative aims to bring together the array of assets under the Cigna Group's umbrella—from medical and pharmacy benefits to behavioral health to specialty care—to address the individual needs of members and patients.

CEO Brian Evanko said during Cigna's earnings call that the program aims to lean on data analytics and technology to identify risks more quickly and deliver personalized supports. The goal, he said, is to drive better outcomes while lowering healthcare costs in tandem.

"Our Lead to One vision will benefit all customers, and we see a particular opportunity with the most clinically complex patient needs, where healthcare costs are increasingly concentrated and represent over 50% of total healthcare spending," he said.

Evanko said that the approach helps to establish "clear strategic growth framework for creating long-term value."

"It is an exciting time here at The Cigna Group as our company unifies around a single vision, Lead to One, and begins to compound the distinctive capabilities we have developed to capture the opportunity set in front of us," he said.

In addition, the company is undertaking an initiative called Accelerate to One, which aims to support operational efficiency for its workforce while supporting greater accountability and better decision-making. Evanko said that program is set to drive $3 billion in run rate savings for the company by 2030.

Evanko said the company expects to see those savings opportunities materialize across the company.

Cigna is the latest health insurance company to boost its guidance for the year on the back of its Q3 results.

The company released its earnings report Thursday morning, posting $1.4 billion in profit. That's down year over year from the $1.9 billion haul the company reported in the third quarter of 2025.

In addition, revenues for the quarter were $73.7 billion, up from $69.7 billion in the prior-year quarter. Both figures surpassed Wall Street analysts' predictions, per Zacks Investment Research.

Through the first three quarters of the year, the company has earned $4.8 billion in profit, on par with the $4.7 billion posted for the first nine months of 2025. Total revenue at the end of Q3 was $213.8 billion, growing from $202.4 billion through three quarters in 2025.

CEO Brian Evanko said in the release that the company's current financial strategy is rooted in three key principles, including "driving core growth, leveraging our distinct capabilities and executing with discipline."

"As we put our strategy in action, our strong third quarter results demonstrate how we are building on this momentum by making health care more affordable and personalized, while advancing Accelerate to One to drive productivity, reduce costs, and support sustained long-term earnings growth," he said.

The company reported a medical loss ratio in the quarter of 85%, up slightly from 84.8% in Q3 2025. Cigna attributed the uptick to higher costs in its employer plans, while lower costs in the individual market segment did partially offset this trend.

Cigna Healthcare, the company's core insurance business, reported 18.45 million members as of Q3. By comparison, the insurer had 18.1 million members at the end of 2025.

Pharmacy benefit membership, meanwhile, was 117.7 million, down 123.6 million as of Dec. 31, 2025. Cigna said the decrease reflects "expected client transitions" as well as lower membership at its health plan clients.

Its Evernorth unit reported $63.8 billion in adjusted revenue for the quarter, up from $61.5 billion a year ago. Highlights include ongoing organic growth in its specialty pharmacy business, the company said.

Cigna Healthcare reported $11.7 billion in adjusted revenue for Q3, up from $10.7 billion in the prior-year quarter. The company said this largely reflects higher premium revenue as it adapts to higher medical costs.

On the back of the results, Cigna elevated its outlook and now expects at least $30.60 in earnings per share for 2026. It also projects a full-year MLR of 84.2%.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/regulatory/cms-final-2027-outpatient-pay-rule-brings-hospitals-little-relief-340b-site-neutral" hreflang="en">CMS’ final 2027 outpatient pay rule brings hospitals little relief on 340B, site-neutral changes</a>

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
CMS’ final 2027 outpatient pay rule brings hospitals little relief on 340B, site-neutral changes" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

The Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services on Tuesday finalized its annual outpatient hospital payments rule with a lower-than-proposed rate bump and its major, industry-opposed policy shifts moderated, but largely intact.

The 2027 Hospital Outpatient Prospective Payment (OPPS) and Ambulatory Surgical Center (ASC) Payment Systems rule outlines a 2.3% reimbursement increase for those meeting applicable quality reporting requirements. That’s below the proposed 2.4% increase and reflects a 3.2% projected hospital market basket increase and a 0.9 percentage point productivity adjustment.

The agency is also moving forward with a substantial and controversial cut in 340B drug reimbursements, though not quite to the extent that it initially proposed.

The final rule adjusts these drug payments down from the current bar of average sales price (ASP) plus 6% to ASP minus 28%, as opposed to the proposed 33.4% below ASP. To make the reimbursement change budget neutral as required by statute, OPPS payments for non-drug services will be boosted by 7.5%.

CMS said it tweaked the proposed 340B reimbursement cut based on 340B ceiling price data from the Health Resources and Services Administration, with the baseline reductions informed by a drug acquisition cost survey it conducted earlier this year. Those changes will reduce Original Medicare drug payments by $4.35 billion and the drug payments from beneficiaries by $1.1 billion (or $900 on average per beneficiary who receives an affected drug from a hospital outpatient department) in the policy’s first year, CMS said.

The rate adjustment did little to calm hospital industry groups who’d railed against the 340B reimbursement changes as a threat to safety-net care and a gift to for-profit hospitals that do not participate in the subsidy program but would benefit from the budget-neutral offset. The American Hospital Association, the Association of American Medical Colleges and America’s Essential Hospitals each condemned the finalized policy in reaction statements, with the latter two issuing more forceful rebukes.

“CMS has based its reimbursement to 340B hospitals on the results of a hasty and faulty survey, failing to follow the clear requirements of the Medicare statute,” AAMC Chief Health Care Officer Jonathan Jaffery, M.D., said. “The drug payment cuts disproportionately affect and target non-profit and public hospitals serving low-income patients, while redistributing billions of dollars to hospitals that do not share the same safety-net and academic missions. These cuts threaten access to critical services made possible by the 340B program and will lead to worse health outcomes for patients.”

“Rather than strengthening and preserving safety net funding, CMS is instead prioritizing hospitals that do not serve the safety net and nodding to the pharmaceutical industry's interests,” AEH’s statement reads. “These cuts are landing at a time when essential hospitals and their mission are more important than ever, as millions of people risk losing their Medicaid coverage in the coming months.”

The Federation of American Hospitals, which represents for-profit hospitals that do not participate in 340B, struck a different tone, calling the rulemaking “a win for patients, taxpayers and the hospitals that communities depend on.”

“By bringing Medicare drug payments more in line with actual costs, this reform will lower seniors’ out-of-pocket expenses and redirect billions in Medicare dollars where they’re needed most—supporting access to care for patients served by small and rural hospitals across the country,” Charlene MacDonald, the group’s president and CEO, said in a statement. “We applaud the Administration for advancing a smarter, fairer approach to Medicare payment.”

Additionally, CMS finalized an increase in an annual OPPS conversion factor to non-drug items and services, speeding up the agency’s effort to offset a prior $7.8 billion recoupment to 340B hospitals it was forced to make after previously reducing 340B rates contrary to law. But while this accelerated payback—often described by non-340B hospitals as a clawback—was proposed as an increase from 0.5% to 3%, the final rule brings the conversion factor to 2%.

AHA Senior Vice President of Public Policy Analysis and Development Ashley Thompson described both 340B policy changes together as a “continued assault” on the subsidy program that “will further stress hospitals’ ability to care for their patients.” AAMC’s Jaffery said the combined policies’ impact “will be devastating to patients,” and that his group “remains committed to pursuing all available avenues to reverse these payment cuts.”

Further rankling the hospital industry was the final rule’s continued push toward site-neutral payment policy and a phase-out of the inpatient-only list.

For the former, the agency will apply Physician Fee Schedule payment rates for some imaging services to off-campus provider-based departments. In line with the proposed rule, CMS said it expects the plan to trigger first-year reductions in Medicare Part B expenditures and beneficiary cost-sharing obligations of $260 million and $70 million, while also reducing a market incentive for provider consolidation.

Meanwhile, CMS finalized its proposal to remove 647 services from the inpatient-only list, allowing them to be delivered and paid for in the lower-cost outpatient setting.

“Both policies are misguided,” AHA’s Thompson said, “and overlook the unique role hospital outpatient departments play in delivering care to patients in communities nationwide. Hospital outpatient departments treat sicker and more complex groups of patients than independent physician practices, including those with higher rates of disability and who are more likely to come from low-income and rural communities.”

CMS’ fact sheet on the 1,270-page annual rule also noted various changes to quality reporting programs for hospital outpatient departments and ASCs, a proposal to require prior authorization for eight botulinum toxin injection codes CMS said it has seen a jump in claim volume, and new permission for accreditation organizations to assess hospitals’ compliance with EMTALA (the Emergency Medical Treatment and Labor Act of 1986).

“CMS is committed to spending taxpayer funds wisely and efficiently, so [that] America’s seniors can continue getting the care they need,” CMS Administrator Mehmet Oz, M.D., said in a release. “By right-sizing payments and rooting out waste, today’s reforms will drive affordability in Medicare for current beneficiaries while ensuring it will still be there to serve their children and grandchildren.”

Stoke City and Middlesbrough have struck gold with American agreements

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Stoke City and Middlesbrough are absolutely flying in the Championship right now, and the key to their recent success is more obvious than some people may realise.

Stoke and Middlesbrough both look like real top-eight contenders at this stage, as the Potters make it just one loss in their last 11 league games following victory at Blackburn Rovers on Tuesday night, whilst Boro retain their place at the top of the table.

Both underwent badge changes over the summer, and both are in scintillating form right now, though those aren't the only similarities between the two clubs.

Stoke underwent another busy summer window, as the likes of Sorba Thomas, Lewis Baker, Steven Nzonzi, and Tatsuki Seko left the club, whilst Ethan Galbraith, Djibril Soumare, Luca Bombino, and Jack McGlynn were just a handful of arrivals.

Bombino, 20, arrived from Californian-based outfit San Diego FC for around $5 million (£3.7 million) to provide competition in both wing-back positions, whilst McGlynn, 23, joined in a deal worth around $5.5 million (£4.1 million), including add-ons, from MLS outfit Houston Dynamo to add another option in attacking midfield.

Both arrived late in the window, with little known about them to Stoke fans, as it now looks as though the Potters have taken a trick out of Middlesbrough's book to great effect.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Middlesbrough endured, on paper, one of the best summer transfer windows the Championship has ever seen, with each of Hayden Hackney, Abdoulaye Kante, and Morgan Whittaker departing, as well as the sell-on clause for Morgan Rogers' move from Aston Villa to Chelsea; Boro raked in around £40 million from player sales.

With that money, each of Will Lankshear, Kyle Joseph, Radek Vitek, Ashley Phillips, Sebastian Berhalter, Rokas Pukstas, and Max Arfsten arrived at the Riverside, many for potentially lucrative deals, though Kim Hellberg's side remains in the green despite this outlay.

Berhalter joined from Vancouver Whitecaps for around $1.5 million (£1 million), Pukstas from Hajduk Split for around £4.3 million, and Arfsten from Columbus Crew for around $7.5 million (£5.6 million), with each joining compatriot Aidan Morris on Teesside.

Now, a few months into the campaign, the Americans at Middlesbrough and Stoke are showing exactly why they are an untapped and underrated market right now.

McGlynn and Bombino have been regulars in the Stoke side since arriving in ST4, with Bombino rotating with Eric Bocat, Ben Johnson, and Junior Tchamadeu at wing-back, whilst McGlynn has nailed down a spot as one of the attacking midfielders in the Potters' starting XI, with four goals and two assists in ten Championship games so far.

McGlynn appears to be having his own Goal of the Season competition at the moment, with excellent strikes against Watford, Sheffield United, Preston North End, and most recently Blackburn Rovers, and looks to be a real star at this level.

With McGlynn and Bombino shining at Stoke, Berhalter, in particular, is having a real impact at Middlesbrough right now, playing alongside compatriot Morris in the middle of the park, with three goals and two assists in 13 league games thus far.

Arfsten and Pukstas haven't been as fortunate with minutes at this stage, but there is every chance they come good and have the desired impact in due course, as Boro led Stoke down the American market, and both teams are reaping the rewards right now.

What do you make of Middlesbrough and Stoke exploring the American market? Is this something teams in the EFL ought to be doing more? Share your thoughts below!

With Middlesbrough and Stoke already seeing their investment in the American market pay dividends, it wouldn't be surprising if more clubs around the EFL began to follow suit.

The MLS has developed some gems over the years that have gone on to forge excellent careers across Europe, and for relatively cheap a lot of the time, there are a lot of potential diamonds ready to test themselves across the pond.

Middlesbrough led the way; Stoke have followed, both are reaping the rewards, and sooner or later, the American market will become a conveyor belt for players heading to Europe.

Berkshire Hathaway's Stock Portfolio Is Worth About 3 Times What It Paid. The Gains Alone Equal About 19% of the Company.

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Berkshire's capital gains on investments it sells work like the typical investor's capital gains taxes do. Just don't expect to notice it if you're a shareholder.

Anyone familiar with Berkshire Hathaway (BRKA +3.93%) (BRKB +3.95%) probably knows it owns a bunch of individual stocks largely hand-picked by former CEO Warren Buffett, or more recently, with some input from current CEO Greg Abel.

Have you ever thought about how these holdings are taxed though? It's not like a mutual fund or exchange-traded fund, which require a yearly reckoning. As a C corporation, Berkshire's stock holdings are taxed much like an individual investor's are. That is, the capital gains tax bill is incurred for the tax year in which the asset is sold.

Regardless, there's a pretty big future tax liability sitting on Berkshire's books right now. Just don't worry about it if you're a shareholder. It's unlikely you'll even notice when the time comes to sell any of these positions.

As of the middle of this year, Berkshire Hathaway was sitting on $323.8 billion worth of individual stocks, including Coca-Cola, Apple, and American Express. It only paid a total of $106.5 billion for these picks over the course of the past many years, however, translating into unrealized net gains of $217.3 billion that will eventually become realized, taxable gains. For perspective on that figure, Berkshire's total market cap at the time was $1.1 trillion. All of these numbers have changed somewhat since then, although not by much.

Current shareholders don't need to worry too much about capital gains taxes chipping away at the conglomerate's net profits, however. Like his predecessor, Abel moves big positions around pretty slowly, using time to the company's full advantage.

And this raises the question: If its stock portfolio is currently worth $361.3 billion, while the organization's market cap stands at nearly $1.2 trillion, what accounts for the remaining $828.7 billion in Berkshire Hathaway's market valuation?

Another $365.5 billion of it is cash or near-cash equivalents, such as short-term government bonds. That means the other $463.2 billion reflects the perceived value of all the privately owned businesses that are part of the Berkshire Hathaway family, including Clayton Homes, insurer GEICO, Duracell batteries, and Dairy Queen, just to name a few.

Data source: Berkshire Hathaway. Chart by author.

Yes, you're seeing that right -- Berkshire's stocks are the smallest piece of its total value, and the businesses you rarely hear about are the largest.

Moreover, this $463.2 billion in wholly owned enterprises is on pace to produce on the order of $48 billion in operating earnings this fiscal year. From an income-based perspective, these privately owned companies may still be collectively undervalued by the market, even if their typical profit-growth rates are in the modest single digits.

But that big looming tax bill? Again, don't worry too much about it. It's unlikely you'll even notice it if and when the time comes. The conglomerate's sold out several positions this year alone, and investors didn't bat an eye. In fact, Berkshire's unrealized net gains were also $202.7 billion five years ago, when its stock portfolio was worth $307.9 billion. Plenty of those positions, however, have changed in the meantime.

American Express is an advertising partner of Motley Fool Money. James Brumley has positions in Coca-Cola. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends American Express, Apple, and Berkshire Hathaway. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

GomSpace sets financial target to reach revenue of SEK 1.6 billion by 2030

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

GomSpace has set medium term financial targets to reach SEK 1.6 billion in annual revenue by 2030 and achieve an EBITDA margin above 20% over the same period.

STOCKHOLM, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Building on its transformed operating model, improved profitability, stronger market position and expanding geographic presence, GomSpace has set new financial targets, for revenue reaching SEK 1.6 billion in annual revenue by 2030, implying a CAGR of at least 30% from last twelve-month (LTM) Q3 2026 through 2030. The goal reflects the company's intention to gain market share through GomSpace' integrated systems capabilities, continued international expansion and greater participation in higher-value solution and constellation programs.

Alongside its revenue target, GomSpace aims to reach an EBITDA margin above 20% by 2030. The company expects economies of scale, increased standardization, greater industrialization and continued product innovation to support this goal, potentially complemented by inorganic initiatives to enhance competitiveness. To support the pace and scale of its planned growth, GomSpace during the period expects to supplement internally generated funds with additional capital.

CEO Carsten Drachmann comments: "Over the past three and a half years, we have transformed GomSpace and built a disciplined foundation for profitable growth, continuous improvement and stronger investor trust, including a significant increase in no of shareholders and market cap.

We are now entering our next phase where we intend to capitalize on this transformation and the position we have created and together with additional strategic investments into the operations aim to create long-term shareholder value through ambitious, profitable growth.

This will require larger contracts, expansion into new markets and further investment in technology, business models and our portfolio. As larger opportunities involve longer sales cycles and create value over several years, results may vary between periods, but I remain confident about the long-term path. We will assess progress over time against our new financial targets.

People, values and culture will remain fundamental to consistent execution as we scale."

Background to the financial targetsThe market for small satellites below 500 kg is expected to grow significantly in the coming years. According to Extrapolate, the market is projected to increase from USD 3.79 billion in 2024 to USD 9.58 billion in 2031, corresponding to a CAGR of 14.2%. Although this forecast does not precisely match every market segment served by GomSpace, it provides a relevant benchmark: achieving our target will require both market growth and sustained gains in market share.

Over the past three years, GomSpace has transformed its operating model by establishing dedicated business units with clear market focus, accountability and go-to-market responsibility. This structure provides a scalable platform for profitable growth and has demonstrated the company's ability to increase revenue while improving profitability.

On a rolling 12-month basis, revenue increased from SEK 257 million in Q3 2024 to SEK 515 million in Q3 2026, representing an annualized growth rate of approximately 42% over the two-year period. At the end of that period, GomSpace also delivered an EBITDA margin above 10%.

The EBITDA margin target to reach above 20% is supported by expected scale benefits, increased standardization, greater industrialization and continued product innovation. The margin may vary depending on the mix of business between the business units.

Growth and profitability may vary from quarter to quarter and year to year, but the company believes its medium-term financial targets can be achieved through disciplined execution of its business-unit strategy, expansion of its international commercial footprint and deeper penetration of existing and adjacent markets. Investments in scale, industrialization and innovation will remain priorities for sustainable growth and competitiveness.

The business units Products and Satellite Systems, GomSpace's two largest business units, are expected to form the core of the growth trajectory going forward. Growth well above the market is expected to come from repeat business with existing customers and expansion into new regions, providing a substantial share of the growth required to achieve the medium-term financial targets.

Advanced Missions focus on emerging technologies and mission areas, including Space Domain Awareness, very low Earth orbit (VLEO), and lunar and deep-space exploration. The business unit develops technologies and capabilities that position GomSpace for future growth through European defense initiatives, space agency programs worldwide, and public and private exploration missions. It is expected to grow in line with the market and remain profitable, while prioritizing strategic technology development over near-term profit maximization. Participation in larger European programs, faster technology adoption, or accelerated lunar and deep-space activity could provide upside to the targets.

The North America business unit is expected to grow well above the market and make a meaningful contribution to achieving the financial targets and the Company expects progressively stronger market traction through 2030; earlier or more rapid acceleration would represent upside potential.

The business unit National & Defense Solutions targets high-value sovereign programs that can generate larger, longer-duration contracts and strengthen long-term backlog visibility. From a limited revenue base today, the unit is expected to contribute progressively alongside the other business units toward the SEK 1.6 billion revenue target by 2030.

GomSpace expects a balanced build-up across the business units, rather than reliance on any single business unit or award; earlier or larger contract wins would represent significant upside potential, although timing remains difficult to predict because sales cycles are long.In summaryGomSpace is targeting at least 30% annual revenue growth through 2030, SEK 1.6 billion in annual revenue, and an EBITDA margin reaching above 20% over the same period.

The new financial targets are thus based on continued execution across the company's established business units, with Products and Satellite Systems expected to form the core growth engine. Earlier market acceleration or larger contract wins provide upside potential, while progress will be assessed over time against the medium-term targets.

About GomSpace Group ABFounded in 2007, GomSpace is a global provider of small satellite solutions with customers in more than 60 countries. The company's business operations are mainly conducted through the wholly owned Danish subsidiary, GomSpace A/S, with headquarters and operational facilities in Aalborg, Denmark. GomSpace also has key operations in Luxembourg, France, and the United States. GomSpace develops and delivers advanced systems and services that enable governments, commercial enterprises, and research institutions to achieve their objectives in space. The company's expertise covers satellite subsystems, complete small satellite missions, and satellite operations, providing solutions that support smarter, faster, and more affordable access to space. The company is listed on Nasdaq First North Premier Growth Market in Stockholm under the ticker GOMX. FNCA Sweden AB is the Company's Certified Adviser. Lakers star Luka Doncic completes unprecedented scoring feat in win vs. Kings

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Lakers star Luka Doncic completes unprecedented scoring feat in win vs. Kings appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Luka Doncic and the Los Angeles Lakers took care of business against the Sacramento Kings on Wednesday night, and the Lakers superstar made some more NBA history along the way.

Doncic poured in 40 points in the 116-103 win at Golden 1 Center. In the process, he became the first player in NBA history to put together six straight games with at least 35 points, five rebounds, five assists and five made 3-pointers, according to OptaSTATS.

The Slovenian star got it done efficiently against Sacramento. He shot 11-of-23 from the field, including 6-of-13 from deep, while knocking down 12 of his 13 free throws. He also grabbed nine rebounds and dished out seven assists, though he did commit six turnovers.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Doncic took over in the second quarter. With the game tied at 46, he ripped off 11 straight points, starting with a jumper over Leaky Black and following it up with three 3-pointers, including one over Darius Acuff Jr. That burst gave the Lakers a 57-46 lead, and Doncic went into halftime with 23 points.

Austin Reaves added 21 points and four assists, while Walker Kessler chipped in 15 points, eight rebounds, four blocks and three steals. For the Kings, Zach LaVine scored 21 points, and Domantas Sabonis posted 16 points and 17 rebounds.

The historic stretch has been nothing short of remarkable. Just two games ago, Doncic dropped 45 points, 15 rebounds, six assists and five steals while drilling eight 3-pointers against the Toronto Raptors, becoming the only player ever to reach all of those marks in a single game. He followed that up with 42 points, 13 assists and seven rebounds against the Miami Heat, hitting six threes in that one as well.

With Wednesday's win, the Lakers improved to 7-2 on the season, pulling into a tie with the Houston Rockets for the best record in the Western Conference. Sacramento, meanwhile, fell to 1-7. Los Angeles has now won three in a row since a loss to the Golden State Warriors.

Fittingly, the streak began in a loss to the Portland Trail Blazers on Oct. 27, when Doncic racked up 41 points, nine assists, five rebounds and six 3-pointers. He will get a chance to extend it to seven against that same Blazers squad, which sits at 6-1, when Los Angeles hosts Portland on Friday.

India news: ​​Several feared trapped as building collapses in Old Delhi's Chawri Bazar

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Rescue operations are ongoing as a building collapsed in Chawri Bazaar. Meanwhile, "Ramayana," starring Ranbir Kapoor and Sai Pallavi, has hit theaters. Follow DW for more.

Here is a roundup of the biggest headlines from across India on Thursday, November 5.

Two people were killed when a building under construction collapsed in Old Delhi's Chawri Bazaar on Thursday morning, local media reported. A third person was pulled out injured.

The collapse happened in Gali Sitara, near Chawri Bazaar Metro station. The first alerts came around 10 a.m. local time. Six fire units were sent to the site, and it was initially unclear how many people had been inside.

The accident hit a crowded wholesale market amid the festive shopping rush. It comes two months after a building collapsed in the crowded Satya Niketan area frequented by college students, killing seven people.

All 11 people reported missing after two rescue boats capsized in a flooded lake in Telangana's Khammam district have been found safe, officials said Thursday, according to local media.

"One person was rescued at around 2.15 am (local time), followed by four others at approximately 3 am. We subsequently located three more personnel, and they were brought to shore. The operation was suspended temporarily and was resumed at 5 am. In the next couple of hours, all the remaining stranded persons were rescued," Khammam district fire officer Ajay Kumar told the Hindustan Times.

Those rescued include State Disaster Response Force (SDRF) personnel, fire services department members and a local resident.

The boats overturned Wednesday evening in the swollen Bethupalli lake near Sathupalli. The crews were reportedly trying to reach fishermen stranded by rising water after heavy rain. Strong currents flipped both SDRF boats.

Early accounts put the missing at 10, but later reports raised the figure to 11. Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy asked officials to step up efforts to bring the rescuers back safely.

Champions League power rankings: PSG remain a joke – but who is catching them?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Paris Saint-Germain and Real Madrid are the only teams to still have 100 per cent records after Liverpool’s defeat at Fenerbahce

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

It was largely business as usual in matchweek four of the Champions League as Paris Saint-Germain and Barcelona kept on scoring and Arsenal and Bayern Munich returned to winning ways while remaining unbeaten.

But the story of the round belonged to Como, who made history by beating Lens 9-1 away from home and equalling the record for the most goals scored in a single Champions League game. Cesc Fabregas’s impressive side now sit fourth in the league phase standings, behind PSG, Real Madrid and Arsenal.

Meanwhile, there was a frustrating defeat for Liverpool at Fenerbahce, but Manchester United produced a stunning comeback to beat Roma and potentially save Michael Carrick’s job, with Matheus Cunha scoring twice on a remarkable night at Old Trafford.

We compiled our 36-team power rankings by combining team’s previous performance in the Champions League, their results in the competition so far, and the likelihood of them going all the way this season.

Agree or disagree with our standings? Let us know and have your say in the comments below.

The reigning champions are making a mockery of suggestions that they are slow starters in the Champions League. Luis Enrique’s back-to-back champions finished outside the top eight in both of their previous successes but have started this campaign on fire in Europe. PSG were 4-0 up after just 17 minutes at Villarreal, and that was without Ousmane Dembele and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia. The French champions will be without the brilliant Georgian for the rest of the season after he suffered a ruptured ACL but they remain the team to beat.

Defeats to Paris Saint-Germain in the Champions League and Real Madrid in LaLiga ended their winning start under Hansi Flick and perhaps raised the same concerns over whether their defence holds up come the knockout stages. But Lamine Yamal is arguably the world’s best player on current form and Raphinha isn’t too bad, either. The Brazilian made it an astonishing 19 goals and three assists in just 13 matches across LaLiga and the Champions League with his double in the 3-0 win over Aston Villa at Camp Nou.

Mikel Arteta’s got the result they needed in Prague, even if a difficult second-half performance made it a bit of a struggle. An entertaining draw last time out at Bayern Munich, who have such a strong home record, gave a good account of whether the Premier League champions can take the next step against a heavyweight side in the business end of the Champions League.

Bayern returned to winning ways with a professional win against Atletico Madrid – and the big surprise was that Harry Kane was not on the scoresheet. The Ballon d’Or holder’s 10-match scoring run in the Champions League came to an end at the Metropolitano, missing the chance to equal Cristiano Ronaldo’s record. But Bayern rolled on.

Perhaps chaos is the new normal under Jose Mourinho. Real Madrid trailed late on in Athens but Kylian Mbappe equalised before Fede Valverde scored a stunning winner in stoppage time. Jude Bellingham also scored an outrageous flick. Somehow with maximum points, despite trailing Roma 3-1 at the Stadio Olimpico, and Inter and RB Leipzig spurning chances to take the lead at the Bernabeu, the 15-time Champions League winners continue to find a way.

Back-to-back defeats in the Premier League have the shine off an impressive start for Enzo Maresca’s side, while uncertainty off the pitch remains as well. But a battling win at RB Leipzig, with a customary Erling Haaland goal, was a good response, even if City have now gone seven games without a clean sheet. Next up: the return of Kevin De Bruyne with Napoli, followed by a mouthwatering trip to Barcelona.

Liverpool have conceded three goals in three consecutive games in the Champions League or European Cup for the first time and the manner of their “messy” collapse against Fenerbahce was a concern. The nature of Liverpool’s squad means Andoni Iraola has needed to rotate in Champions League but Liverpool’s performance in Istanbul underlined the task he faces. In-form Club Brugge can’t be taken lightly after the international break.

Despite defeat in the Camp Nou, you get the feeling that Unai Emery’s new-look Aston Villa will have learned a lot from their meeting with Barcelona. They had moments of threat, too. Bright showings from the refreshed attacking line-up of Nicolas Jackson, Johan Manzambi, Ibrahim Mbaye and Alysson have helped the Europa League winners retaining their identity under Emery despite the huge squad turnover in the summer.

After starting with back-to-back defeats, how badly Inter needed to respond with back-to-back wins. A comfortable win at Feyenoord also came after Saturday’s Milan derby defeat, with Pio Esposito stepping up amid Lautaro Martinez’s continued absence.

WOW. If Como announced themselves by thrashing Manchester United, the Champions League debutants stole the show this week with a NINE-goal dismantling of poor Lens. Cesc Fabregas’s stylish side became the first team to score nine goals away from home in the Champions League or European Cup since 1971. The Italian side are certainly making a name for themselves. Those games against PSG and Barcelona at the end of the league phase are going to be fascinating.
