Tottenham Must Do Everything To Keep Barcelona Target Pedro Porro

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Barcelona are 'dreaming' of signing Tottenham defender Pedro Porro, according to reports in Spain.

Porro, 27, who joined Spurs from Sporting CP for £41million in January 2023, has four-and-a-half years left on his contract in north London, having signed a bumper new deal in the summer. During his time at Tottenham, the Spaniard has made 158 appearances, scoring 13 goals and providing 26 assists, and is a crucial member of Roberto De Zerbi's squad.

But, according to Mundo Deportivo, Barca are keen on Porro and have made him a 'dream' target for the right-back position. The La Liga side want him to re-unite with Lamine Yamal on that side, with the duo forming a strong partnership in the Spain national team.

Hansi Flick's side are aware that it's 'unlikely' they can sign him in January, but they aren't closing the door on a winter move. Mundo add that if an opportunity were to arise, then Barcelona would pounce on it.

Tottenham value Porro at around £60million but Barca would want to drive the price down given their financial situation. Real Madrid, meanwhile, are among the others keeping a close eye on Porro's future at Tottenham.

Last week, Porro admitted he would "love" to return to Spain at some point, having spent time at Girona and Real Valladolid earlier in his career. “Obviously, I’d love to return to Spain someday," he said.

"It’s my country, and I like the league. Right now, it would be very unfair to talk about anything other than my club because I’m under contract, but obviously, I’d love to go back to Spain; it’s a big dream of mine.”

It's imperative that Tottenham keep Porro at the club, despite interest from Barcelona and Real Madrid, if they want to return to the Champions League, at least, next season. Although he has been in and out of the side this season due to injuries and fitness issues, he is still one of Tottenham's most important players, and integral for De Zerbi.

Archie Gray has done a stellar job filling in, and even scored the opener in the 2-0 win against Crystal Palace on Saturday, but he doesn't possess the same level of control and ability to both make a line-breaking pass and deliver an inch-perfect on-point cross.

Gray is a work-horse and is arguably a better one-v-one defender than Porro, but Tottenham are a much more complete side and can offer more from an attacking perspective when Porro is on the pitch.

Although Spurs could dip into the market to replace Porro, there isn't many like him. Trent Alexander-Arnold, one of the best defenders in world football, is a similar mould, but a move for him to leave Real Madrid and move to Tottenham would be doubtful.

Therefore, Tottenham have to keep Porro - even if they bid more than his valuation - as he is their best right-back and gives them so much more than that. His attacking potential is so crucial, having contributed to 39 goals in three-and-a-half years, as well his ability to drive up the pitch and control matches from his position.

Tottenham youngster Luca Williams-Barnett came off the bench against Crystal Palace to make a real impact, despite being on the pitch for just a few m

Should Investors Buy Into the Chipotle Turnaround?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Chipotle continued its turnaround with another quarter of positive same-store sales growth.

Chipotle Mexican Grill (CMG +5.83%) continued its recent turnaround with its third straight quarter of comparable-store sales growth. The solid results lifted the stock nearly 9% on Oct. 29, although its shares are still down more than 15% on the year.

Let's dig into the fast-casual Tex-Mex restaurant operator's quarterly results and prospects to see if the stock's rebound can continue.

After seeing declining same-store sales growth throughout much of last year, Chipotle has now turned in positive same-store sales growth each quarter of 2026. This started with a modest 0.5% increase in the first quarter, and then a 2.2% rise in the second. It followed those gains with a 2.1% comparable-store increase in the third quarter.

Transactions rose 0.6%, while it said pricing was up about 2.7%. It started to see industry headwinds in mid-July that slowed its momentum, but trends normalized in late August. It then saw solid momentum pick up after the launch of Pollo Asado and Chili Lime Chips in September, although October trends have softened.

Chipotle also saw a boost from its reward program, which it revamped in April. It now has over 24 million active members, and it is seeing strong in-store sign-ups. It said over 33% of its sales now come from loyalty members.

Importantly, the company said that it expects its same-store sales momentum to pick up starting next month. It is looking for comparable-store sales to climb by 2% or more in the fourth quarter, with an exit rate for the year around 3% to 4%. The growth is expected to be fueled by a combination of marketing initiatives and the November launch of its popular brisket limited-time offering.

Overall, Chipotle's revenue climbed by nearly 10% to $3.29 billion in the quarter, while its adjusted earnings per share (EPS) fell 7% to $0.27. That was generally in line with analysts' expectations for EPS of $0.29 on sales of $3.29 billion, according to LSEG.

Restaurant-level operating margin fell by 120 basis points to 23.3%. This is one of the most important metrics in the restaurant industry, as it shows how profitable individual restaurants are. The company has been feeling pressure from wage inflation, higher labor usage, and increased prices, and as such, it plans to raise prices by 3% in Q4.

Chipotle continues to add new restaurants, opening 84 company-owned locations in the quarter and five international ones with partners. During the quarter, it opened restaurants in Mexico, Saudi Arabia, and South Korea. It said Mexico has exceeded expectations, while Saudi Arabia opening volumes set a new record.

It plans to add between 350 and 370 new restaurants in 2026, with between 10 and 15 of them in international markets with partners. It ended the quarter with 4,267 restaurants.

Looking ahead, Chipotle plans to add another 340 to 360 U.S. and Canada company-owned locations and 15 to 25 international company-owned and partner-operated restaurants in 2027.

Chipotle continues to show signs of turning around its business with solid same-store sales growth. However, inflation continues to run high, and its sales growth has not yet reached a level to help it stabilize its restaurant-level margins. This is leading to reduced earnings power.

A 3% price increase set for the end of 2026 and 2027, combined with increased traffic, could be a recipe for improvements in this area, which is what you really want to see to get excited about the stock. Until then, an investment in the company is more about its turnaround potential than its growth.

With the stock trading at a forward price-to-earnings (P/E) multiple of 23 times 2027 analyst estimates, Chipotle's stock looks appropriately valued in my book. Whether it is a buy is all going to come down to execution. At this time, given the uncertainties, I'd prefer not to chase this recent rally.

Geoffrey Seiler has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Chipotle Mexican Grill. The Motley Fool recommends the following options: short December 2026 $38 calls on Chipotle Mexican Grill. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Companies need to fortify supply chains for “a flock of black swan” events, says Toll Group executive chairman Thomas Knudsen

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Companies must build supply chains for robustness, not resilience, because “a black swan event has become a flock of black swans,” Thomas Knudson, executive chairman of logistics company Toll Group, said Tuesday.

Supply chain shocks have piled up since 2020, following the COVID pandemic, Russia’s 2022 invasion of Ukraine, Trump’s 2025 tariffs and the end of de minimis, and finally the U.S. war with Iran and the closed Strait of Hormuz.

Before 2020, Knudsen said resilience meant reacting—waiting for a crisis, then calling for help. “And then we call our banks, our logistics companies, and we say, ‘Hey, can you help us get out of this problem?’” he said at the Fortune Southeast Asia 500 Summit in Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam.

“Now suddenly you’re looking at your tier two, three, and four suppliers,” he added. “We’ve got to build a supply chain that’s much more robust.” Toll Group, founded in 1888 and currently owned by Japan Post, runs freight forwarding and contract logistics across Asia.

In practice, robustness means spare capacity: backup routes by truck and rail alongside ocean and air, and sea-air combinations that fly cargo part of the way and ship it the rest. “Today we’re doing trucking. Tomorrow we’re doing rail. We’re complementing with ocean and air,” Knudsen explained.

But robustness has limits. For low-value goods like cement, he noted, building a resilient supply chain is difficult simply because of the cost.

Tariffs have also forced Chinese companies to look to new markets and manufacturing hubs. “Is that going to move back? I don’t think so,” Knudsen said.

Standard Chartered’s Future of Trade report—a survey of 2,100 senior corporate leaders across 27 markets—reports that ASEAN executives citing geo-economic actions among their top three trade concerns has risen from 49% over the past year to 57% over the next three to five years.

James Nesbitt, managing director and head of global subsidiaries for Singapore and ASEAN at Standard Chartered, said the bank’s corporate clients are making long-term strategic commitments to the region.

“This isn’t simply about a foreign company setting up a factory in Indonesia, waiting for that factory to produce, and then kind of moving on,” Nesbitt said.

Established in 1992, the ASEAN Free Trade Area has fostered regional integration by eliminating tariff barriers among Southeast Asian countries. ASEAN’s 11 economies together are worth about $4.5 trillion and growing about 4.5% a year, according to the IMF—yet only about 22% of the bloc’s trade happens within it, versus roughly 60% for the European Union.

Knudsen pointed to the EU’s single currency and open borders as the model for ASEAN. “We saw when the UK stepped out of the EU on Brexit, that friction became real,” he said. “It wasn’t just a little obstacle.”

Nesbitt said ASEAN’s diversity could also create opportunities to connect its payment systems. Each market has its own real-time payment network, he said, and an integrated overlay could enable them to work together.

“What if there was an opportunity not to create bilateral ties between ASEAN, but instead have an overlay where all of these different payment infrastructures can operate real time?” he asked. “Can blockchain solve that? Is there an opportunity for tokenization? Or stablecoins to solve for that?”

Ranking the 8 EFL managers most likely to lose their job next

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The sacking season in the EFL is in full swing, and as we approach another international break, the number of gaffers losing their jobs is expected to increase.

Just five minutes before I started writing this, the news out of West Ham United that Nuno Espírito Santo had been sacked following back-to-back home defeats broke.

With the Hammers sitting eighth in the Championship, having scored the most goals across all three EFL divisions, you get the feeling that some bosses who are arguably in worse positions have looked at that and started to get a little more uncomfortable in their chairs.

It's a ruthless business, and these eight managers are the likeliest to be following Nuno along the unemployment line if results don't improve anytime soon.

These last couple of months have been quite confusing at Bristol Rovers, especially given all the good work the club had done under Steve Evans to end last season and begin the ongoing campaign.

With four wins in five to open the new League Two term, the Gas had gone on a run of 15 wins in 19 league outings, stretching to the end of last year. Since then, though, it's been one win in nine, with seven defeats amongst them.

Now, a Bristol Rovers side that many expected to be amongst the title challengers this season are 17th after 14 games. There's plenty of time to turn that around, but some concerns are starting to creep in as the club slides down the fourth-tier table.

Evans should have enough credit in the bank, both in terms of the change of form he oversaw towards the back end of last season and with his track record in this division, to be given time to turn things around, hence his position at the foot of this list.

But if these poor results continue, the pressure will start to intensify on the veteran boss.

Charlton Athletic were on top of the world just a little over three months ago. The Addicks were the only side to have won all three of their opening league games, one of which included a 2-1 win at West Ham, and topped the Championship.

Since then, Charlton have gone 10 games without a win. Half of those games have been draws, but they're, as of writing, on a three-game losing run, which included defeats against out-of-form sides Watford and Norwich City.

The underlying numbers haven't been great, especially in an attacking sense, but now they seem to be leaking goals at a more consistent rate than we know Nathan Jones sides to do.

The Welshman, like Steve Evans, has a lot of credit in the bank, given he was the man to oversee their return to the Championship after five years in League One and keep them there last year. However, this is relegation form, and the Addicks may consider a change if it continues.

Rounding out the 'they've got credit in the bank which may give them time' trio to begin this specific list is Wrexham boss Phil Parkinson. Just nine months ago, co-owners Rob Mac and Ryan Reynolds said that their manager had a "job for life," but that could prove to not be the case sometime soon.

The Red Dragons started last season slowly before picking up a head of steam and finishing just two points outside the play-offs. However, that could be put down to large amounts of squad turnover from yet another promotion and a readjustment to a new level.

This season, Wrexham's record after 13 games is worse than what it was last year, and there's much less room for excuses this time around, with a squad already competent enough to push for the play-offs and with more investment in it.

What's even more damaging is that, while results weren't up to scratch this year, you couldn't argue with the fight in the squad. When they lost, they'd bounce back, with wins at Millwall and against Southampton following defeats against Birmingham City and that heavy one at West Ham, respectively.

They didn't do that against Sheffield United, though, and are now staring at a run of games against Middlesbrough and Wolves before the international break. Lose in those, and that's four straight defeats, which could spell the end of Parkinson's legendary reign at the Racecourse.

Watford are undergoing a really troubling period right now. Usually, that leads to a manager being sacked. After all, there have been 22 different men in the dugout since the Pozzo's took over in 2012.

This situation feels different, though. Watford fans are almost united in support for current boss Alessio Dionisi, with their ire directed at the ownership group and other members of the hierarchy.

Meanwhile, Gino Pozzo is blaming their current run of poor results, which has stretched to six defeats in seven and eight defeats in ten, on the players and Dionisi. So the question is, why hasn't the owner, who has a track record of sacking people, gone ahead and actually done it?

Perhaps the owner isn't as naive as some Watford fans think, and knows the outrage which could come with switching bosses at this point. But ultimately, if the Hornets can't go on a run soon and fall into the bottom three, the trigger will likely be pulled.

The League Two table currently leads to pretty grim reading for Rotherham United. The Millers, who have suffered relegation in two of their last three seasons, are sat above the relegation zone on goals scored, and only Crawley Town have a worse PPG.

Novice manager Alex Bruce has had a lot to deal with in his short time at the AESSEAL New York Stadium, having been brought in at the end of June with just 14 senior players confirmed to be under contract from the previous relegation campaign.

He's had to quickly oversee a big recruitment drive while nursing a relegation hangover that famously ex-League One sides struggle with, and ultimately, the underlying numbers aren't awful — they definitely aren't relegation ones.

Ultimately, Bruce is being let down by certain moments, whether that be mindless red cards, missed penalties at key times or a lack of defensive cohesion. Football is a results business, though, and for the Millers, it's seven league games without a win.

The finger will get pointed at the dugout soon enough, and Bruce doesn't have as much credit in the bank as others on this list to perhaps justify faith being put in him.

You can't help but feel slightly sympathetic about the injury crisis that John Eustace is having to deal with currently at Derby County, especially at the back.

The Rams have had to resort to short-term deals for Jack Robinson and Shane Duffy to paper over cracks. Even then, though, it's been wingers Joe Ward and Ryan Hedges at full-back, and full-back Ryan Nyambe (who was loaned out to League One in January last season) at centre-back before those signings.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

However, despite the shortcomings in terms of defensive availability, Eustace's sides are often very resolute and difficult to break down. That hasn't been the case this season, and they've only scored twice in a game twice in 13 outings.

That's led to just one win to begin the campaign, ironically, against the best defensive team per the underlying data in Portsmouth, and each week, it gets harder to blame the injury crisis for the league position at Pride Park.

The benefit of the doubt may be given now. However, if those players start to come back and this form doesn't turn around, John Eustace may be running out of time.

Colchester United are another side whose fanbase isn't onside with the ownership group, but even they can't deny that the football being presented to them isn't on par with anything they'd expect right now.

Danny Cowley's side are winless in 10 league games, after picking up seven points from their opening three. Although those wins over Oldham Athletic and Rochdale haven't exactly aged well as the campaign has gone on.

The U's need to be sold, but if they're a National League club for the first time since the early 1990s, that may make it even harder for Robbie Cowling to find a buyer.

This Saturday, they host seventh-tier side Worcester City in the FA Cup. They're 17th in the Southern Premier Division Central, level on points with their relegation zone. A loss in that one would stretch their winless run in all competitions to 13, and that could be damning for Cowley.

This Blackpool squad is, on paper, too strong to be where they are right now, in yet another relegation battle in League One.

Ian Evatt has skinned the cat already during his year-long stint at Bloomfield Road. He looked dead and buried at half-time against Port Vale last March, with his side behind against a club destined for the drop, but a turnaround sparked a run of seven wins in nine to stay up.

And while they may have lost talisman Ashley Fletcher in the summer, and injuries continue to plague them, that isn't enough of an excuse for what has been a return to battling down near the bottom.

Who do you think will be next to be sacked in the EFL? Let us know in the comments.

The Tangerines give up so many chances, and if not for Bailey Peacock-Farrell, they'd arguably be in a worse position now than the 19th they currently sit.

A win against MK Dons seemed to give him time, but they followed that up with a 4-1 home defeat against Mansfield Town. Then a draw at Sheffield Wednesday, albeit against a side with 10 men for 50 minutes and who were well on top 11 v 11, once again seems to have loosened the collar once more.

But that time seems borrowed, and like with Colchester, an FA Cup tie against non-league opposition in Hornchurch is definitely not what Evatt will want to face next. A loss in that one could prove fatal.

Keith Wyness hits out at Aston Villa and Jamie Carragher over bust-up antics

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Aston Villa took the wrong option by threatening to take legal action against pundit Jamie Carragher for his recent comments.

That is according to former Villa chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who believes that both parties will have seen their reputations damaged by the saga.

This comes after the club threatened to take legal action against Carragher for suggesting that Villa will "never be a Manchester United" during a recent episode of the Overlap.

Wyness believes that while Carragher arguably overstepped the mark with his comments, his former club should have merely ignored the statements.

Instead, chairman Nassef Sawiris threatened to take legal action against the former Liverpool defender, only for him to go back on that plan after Carragher issued an apology.

The saga has garnered plenty of attention in recent days, and Wyness believes that Villa blew the whole thing out of proportion.

Villa's former chief Wyness - who served as CEO at Villa Park between 2016 and 2018 and now runs a consultancy business advising elite clubs - believes that legal action is "never" the right option for clubs to take in these sorts of situations.

The Scottish businessman is firmly against the notion of footballing matters being decided in courtrooms, unless it is absolutely necessary.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider's Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained how neither Carragher nor Villa have emerged well from the situation.

"I think they've both come out of it diminished; in fact, I don't think it was handled particularly well, but I understand it," he said.

"I've been in that situation where you see stuff talked about your club in the media or individuals in your club in the media, and you want to react badly and strongly and sue people and do all the rest of it. It's football.

"And I'm afraid we've got to learn that, you know, it is yesterday's fish and chip paper, the old theory. And also today's podcasts and today's Overlaps are also yesterday's electronic fish and chip papers.

"So I think you know, I think Carragher did overstep it. I don't think he's done himself any favours; I think he looked very poor.

"So I think Villa could have ignored him quite well, but I understand Nassef sticking up for the club as well, but I think out of it all they both come a little bit diminished. Threatening legal action is never a great way to go, and this one would have been a difficult legal action to handle."

Former Villa scout Mick Brown told Football Insider earlier this week that his former club are likely to tie manager Unai Emery down to a new contract to end speculation over his future.

The Spaniard has been heavily linked with the Manchester United job of late, but Villa have no intention of losing him any time soon.

Brown feels that a new deal for Emery would be the perfect "public show" of "commitment" to both him and the project.

The former Arsenal boss is already under contract until 2029, but Brown believes that it would make sense for Villa to extend that further.

How To Complete The Happy Mask Shopkeeper Quest In The Ocarina Of Time Remake

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There's quite a lot to do in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake. Along with the main quest and dungeons, there are a number of side quests you can complete. One of these is tied to the Happy Mask Shopkeeper, who is looking for someone to help him sell his wares.

Only available in the past, here is everything you need to know about who to sell the masks to and what you get for your troubles!

To start the quest, you will need to first be child Link and will need to progress through the story until you have beaten Dodongo's Cavern. Once that's done, go to Hyrule Castle Town and go into the newly opened Mask Shop to the right of the Hyrule Castle entrance. Speak to the Happy Mask Shopkeeper inside, and he will explain that he needs someone to help sell his masks.

Each time you sell a mask, you will return to the shopkeeper to get a new one. Unfortunately, each mask has a very specific buyer, so it's easy not to know who to sell them to if you're not talking to everyone. Additionally, this can only be done as Young Link, so you'll need to go back in time if you never completed this quest before going to the future.

The first mask you get is the Keaton Mask, which conveniently can be sold to the guard in Kakariko Village. Head to this town located in the northeast corner of Hyrule Fields. Once in the town, go north all the way up the steps to the gate that leads to Death Mountain Pass. Equip the Keaton Mask and talk to the guard standing by the gate.

He will offer to buy the mask from you, explaining that his kid will love it. Agree to sell it to him for 15 Rupees and then return to the Happy Mask Shopkeeper. He will take 10 of the Rupees you just earned, leaving you 5 Rupees that are all yours. How magnanimous.

Your next mask is the Skull Mask, which will be sold for 20 Rupees. However, on our way to getting rid of it, we can actually snag a Deku Stick capacity upgrade as well! To start, you will need to make your way all the way back to the Lost Woods. From the Kokori Forest entrance, you will want to go Right, Left, Right, Left, Left.

Following this path will lead you to a meadow where two Business Scrubs will attack you. Deflect their attacks with your shield, then go to the back of the meadow near the wall and drop a bomb at the cracked grey spot on the ground. This will blow a hole open that you can drop into.

Stand on the podium while wearing the mask to trigger a brief cutscene of all the Deku Scrubs admiring your mask. Then approach the larger one and talk to him to obtain the Biggest Deku Stick Bag.

With that out of the way, time to get rid of the Skull Mask! Head back out of the hole and reset the Lost Woods by going through the wrong entrance. Then, re-enter the Lost Woods and go immediately left through the first door.

Before you sell the mask to the Skull Kid, make sure to play Saria's Song for him to earn a Piece of Heart!

In the next area, you will find a lone Skull Kid standing on a tree. Equip the mask and step onto the tree stump to catch the Skull Kid's attention. Speak with him and agree to sell the mask. Unfortunately, he will only pay you 10 Rupees before vanishing. Return to the Happy Mask Shopkeeper and pay him 20 Rupees to gain access to your next mask.

Take the Spooky Mask from the shopkeeper and make your way back to Kakariko Village. This time, go to the Kakariko Graveyard during the daytime to find a young boy walking around the tombstones. Put the mask on and speak to him. He will exclaim how much he loves the mask and will request to buy it from you.

The boy will spend 50 Rupees, so head back to the Happy Mask Shopkeeper and give him 30 Rupees, netting you 20 Rupees from the transaction. At least the kid isn't a cheapskate like the Skull Kid!``

The last step of this quest is to sell the Bunny Hood to someone running around Hyrule Field. You may have noticed this man during your journey, as he will run around the perimeter of Lon Lon Ranch during the day. You will need to stop and talk with him while wearing the mask either during his run or when he is resting in the field at night.

While his route is fairly lengthy, I have found that this man almost always appears out front of Hyrule Castle during sunset. Wait around until the sun is about to set, and you should see him coming from the west. Run up to the man with the hood on and speak with him to sell it for a whopping 200 Rupees!

Once you've sold the mask, return to the Happy Mask Shopkeeper and talk to him. He will be thrilled with your progress and will tell you that you can rent any mask you want for free. To start, he will give you the Mask of Truth, which, when worn, will allow you to actually talk to Gossip Stones instead of hearing them tell you the time.

As a bonus, you can return to where you got the Biggest Deku Stick bag in the Lost Woods with the Mask of Truth. Step on the stage again, and you'll earn a Deku Nut capacity upgrade!

Dark Link is even more of an aura farmer than ever before in the Ocarina of Time remake.

Nintendo Switch 2 sales plunge 41% as price hikes and memory crunch bite

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Sales of Nintendo's flagship Switch 2 plunged in its fiscal second quarter after the Japanese gaming giant rolled out price hikes amid soaring memory costs.

Nintendo said it sold 2.67 million units of the Switch 2 in the three months ended Sept. 30, down 41% from the same period last year. That was also lower than the 3.82 million units sold in the prior quarter.

During the quarter, Nintendo rolled out price increases for the Switch 2 in the U.S., Canada and Japan after hiking the cost of the device in Japan earlier in the year.

Memory, a core component of Nintendo's consoles, continues to face a supply shortage and huge demand, which is driving an unprecedented price surge for the chips.

Still, the Japanese gaming giant stuck to its previous Switch 2 sales forecast of 16.5 million units in the current fiscal year, which ends in March 2027.

Here's how Nintendo did in its fiscal second quarter, compared with LSEG average estimates:

While the Kyoto-headquartered firm has had some strong games this year, including "Tomodachi Life: Living the Dream" and "Pokémon Pokopia", which became a surprise viral hit, there are no major marquee titles.

Investors hope that "The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time," a remake of one of Nintendo's most iconic games, could boost Switch 2 sales in the coming weeks. The game was released on Thursday.

"Nintendo's only real blockbuster this fiscal year is the Ocarina Of Time remake. It's not enough to make investors happy, especially after the device is now as expensive as it is," Serkan Toto, CEO of Kantan Games, told CNBC.

"It's getting harder and harder to sell video game hardware these days, and only hits help. Nintendo must step on the gas and deliver more big games next fiscal year to make sure the Switch 2 momentum continues."

Nintendo also has new Pokémon games slated for release in 2027.

Sources: Nottingham Forest set Morato stance after West Ham decision

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Transfer Correspondent AUTHORITY Former Sky Sports and ESPN FC correspondent who specialises in global recruitment, contract negotiations, and high-velocity transfer breaking news. FOCUS Exclusive reporting, domestic and international transfer markets, and elite-level insights. THE INSIGHT Pete utilises a Global Network of Industry Sources to deliver verified, first-to-market reporting. His “Deep-Lens” analysis connects the dots between boardroom strategy and pitch-level recruitment, providing a level of transfer clarity that goes beyond surface-level rumours.

Nottingham Forest will sell centre-back Morato next summer if he returns from his loan spell at West Ham United, sources have told Football Insider.

The Hammers are hoping to trigger their purchase option for the Brazilian at the end of the season, but are only likely to do so if they get promoted back to the Premier League.

If the capital club fail to achieve that goal, then the 25-year-old will return to the City Ground, where he remains under contract until 2029.

However, sources say that Forest are already planning to sell Morato next summer if he returns, as he has no long-term future at his parent club.

The arrival of Ousmane Diomande and the emergence of Jair Cunha have forced him firmly down the pecking order.

Oliver Glasner and Co. see no place for him back in the East Midlands, and are prepared to offload him as a result.

Morato was signed by Nuno Espirito Santo when he first moved to the City Ground back in 2024, and the Portuguese has been hugely fond of the former Benfica man.

He has featured heavily for West Ham so far this term, already making nine appearances in the early stages of the Championship season.

However, the defender will now be well aware that there is no place waiting for him back at his parent club if the Hammers don't take up their option to buy him.

Morato has never truly been a first-choice selection for Forest, but still played over 1300 minutes for them in the Premier League last season.

He rotated in and out of the team when called upon, and proved to be a reliable figure at the heart of the backline

However, it is now clear that his days at the City Ground are numbered, with Glasner's arrival forcing him further down the pecking order.

Football Insider revealed earlier this week that Forest remain relaxed over Morgan Gibbs-White's future, despite renewed interest from Tottenham ahead of January.

The Reds recently handed the England international a new contracty to make him their highest-paid player, and they have no plans to lose him any time soon.

Sources say that Forest are confident that Gibbs-White will still be part of their squad until at least the end of the season.

The former Wolves ace has endured a slow start to the season, but Forest believe he has the quality to single-handedly drag them up the table.

Vikings OC assures offense is closer than it looks to fixing itself

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Vikings OC assures offense is closer than it looks to fixing itself appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Minnesota Vikings' early-season success has masked some troubling offensive shortcomings, making their 6-1 record less reassuring than it might appear. Their perfect start to the season finally came to an end in Week 8, as they lost 19-15 to the Detroit Lions on the road Sunday.

Beyond the setback, the game exposed several problems in the passing game, especially quarterback Kyler Murray's accuracy and decision-making. However, offensive coordinator Wes Phillips remains confident that the necessary solutions are already available within the team.

Speaking about Murray and the offense as a whole on Tuesday, Phillips said:

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"Yeah, I don't really want to get into overarching — the fact of the matter is, we lost the game against a tough football team on the road. It's a division game. It stings. But we've got to get better moving forward. And, we'll see as the week goes on where we're at in (the quarterback) spot with (director of player) Tyler Williams and his crew. But we've got to get better. We're going to actively pursue every avenue we can to make that happen. I see the plays. I'm absolutely confident (that solutions do exist). I see the football players we have. I see opportunities within games to make plays and be more explosive on offense and make those things happen. I also see a team that's limiting penalties and turnovers. You talk about run game efficiency, and if that picks up. You talk about, if you hit an explosive here or there, and it's a totally different game."

His comments come after another frustratingly inconsistent performance from Murray against Detroit. The 29-year-old completed just 17 of his 29 passing attempts, and his inaccurate throws cost Minnesota at least one potential touchdown drive.

Consistency has remained elusive for Murray throughout the season, with inaccurate throws and hesitation under pressure repeatedly disrupting the offense. His passing production currently stands at 1,022 yards, five touchdowns and three interceptions. His 86.6 passer rating is also the lowest mark of his NFL career.

The Lions took full advantage of Murray's delayed decisions, getting to the quarterback three times. He was also dealing with hamstring tightness, which could have affected his willingness to escape pressure by scrambling.

That injury situation has raised questions about whether backup quarterback Carson Wentz could receive an opportunity to start against the Buffalo Bills on Monday Night Football. Wentz previously performed well in the Vikings' victories over the Green Bay Packers in Week 1 and the Chicago Bears in Week 2.

Minnesota could also explore the trade market if their quarterback situation does not improve. Phillips' commitment to pursuing every available solution has encouraged speculation about potential additions before the November 10 deadline.

For now, the Vikings immediate priority remains correcting their offensive problems with the personnel already available.

Josh Jacobs needs to display more (or any) contrition

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In May 2026, Packers running back Josh Jacobs crossed a line that should never be crossed. And neither he nor his representatives are acting accordingly in what should be an effort to seek redemption.

He pleaded no contest to battery of a woman. He’s fighting in court to keep the video secret. He’s responding to additional allegations through a strident statement from his lawyers attacking the accusers.

The most tone-deaf gesture of all came last Thursday night, when he celebrated a touchdown by pretending to wear, and to be released from, handcuffs.

His implausible explanation, which accused Prime Video of not showing enough of the celebration and attacked the media for interpreting the images with their lyin’ eyes, was contradicted by the full video of the event.

That’s not the way to play this. His no-contest plea is equivalent to saying, “I did it.” He should be contrite. He should be apologetic. He should be seeking redemption.

The Packers continue to stand behind him, primarily because he’s the best running back on the roster and they want to win games. Their reported interest in Colts running back Jonathan Taylor strongly suggests that, if the Packers could get Taylor, the support for Jacobs would immediately evaporate.

Here’s the basic reality. If the Packers were to cut Jacobs, would anyone sign him? The Chiefs took plenty of heat last week for adding kicker Justin Tucker to the practice squad; they may not be inclined to give another second chance to a player whose arrival would compound the negative reaction to Tucker’s arrival.

The reality, in every sport at every level, is obvious. If the player has enough skill and ability, someone will find a way to justify behavior that shouldn’t be tolerated by an industry that relies so heavily on support from the public, both directly through ticket sales and TV ratings and indirectly through taxpayer funding for stadium projects.

Still, Jacobs has done himself no favors by adopting a posture of defiance. He did something no man should ever do. He’s lucky he’s good enough at football to not lose his job over it.

If he was at the bottom of the depth chart in Green Bay, he would have been gone a long time ago.

Everything Coming to Prime Video in November 2026

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Sports fans can catch the four Friday night doubleheaders from November 6 through 27 featuring Emirates NBA Cup 2026 Group Play action, as well as Thursday Night Football. If you want sports with some added drama and fun acting, look out for Nicolas Cage as John Madden when the Madden biopic makes its way to the streaming platform this month. And finally, the most-anticipated sci-fi event of the year that Prime Video and fans have been waiting for, Blade Runner 2099 will surely take your breath away. There’s a lot to look forward to, so here’s your guide to everything coming to Prime Video in November 2026.

The first-ever television production in The Lord of the Rings franchise, The Rings of Power serves as a prequel to The Hobbit and The Lord of the Rings film trilogy and is adapted from J.R.R. Tolkien’s extensive history of Middle-earth, drawn from the appendices of The Lord of the Rings. Set during a time of relative peace, the series covers major events from Middle-earth’s Second Age, including the forging of the legendary rings and Sauron’s rise to power as the new Dark Lord. Morfydd Clark, Robert Aramayo, Benjamin Walker, Ismael Cruz Córdova, and Daniel Weyman reprise their roles for Season 3, with Jamie Campbell Bower, Eddie Marsan, and Simon Pegg as new characters.

The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power has thrilled fans with its opulent production ever since its premiere in 2022. The first and second seasons were highly praised for their earnest tribute to Tolkien’s universe and the innovative expansion of the history of Middle-earth. Rings of Power Season 3 is set five years after the fall of Eregion during the War of the Elves and Sauron, when Sauron secretly begins to craft his weapon of ultimate power that would give him final victory over all Middle-earth.

Inspired by the life and work of American football coach and commentator John Madden, Madden is an all-new biographical sports drama directed and co-produced by David O. Russell. Starring Nicolas Cage as the titular legend, the film chronicles his journey from a Super Bowl-winning partnership with Al Davis and the Oakland Raiders to becoming football's most recognizable voice and building the worldwide Madden NFL video game series. Christian Bale, Kathryn Hahn, John Mulaney, Sienna Miller, and Shane Gillis star as other real-life personalities.

Described officially as “a celebration of a brilliant mind whose once-in-a-century spirit and personality, partnership with his daring wife Virginia, lifelong friendships, and infectious love of people and football transcended the world of sports,” Madden is a must-watch for sports fans. The Pro Football Hall of Fame NFL coach and sports commentator is one of the most recognizable figures in football history and a beloved icon who famously never had a losing season and has won 16 Sports Emmy Awards. Madden will make its streaming premiere on Prime Video on November 18, following its theatrical release on November 11, 2026.

Created by Ben Watkins and produced by Lonnie Ali and Michael B. Jordan, The Greatest is the first-ever authorized scripted series about Muhammad Ali, presenting an intimate exploration of his phenomenal life as a boxer, humanitarian, and global icon. Jaleen Best stars as the titular legend (who fans might recognize from All American: Homecoming), while showrunner Watkins is best known for creating shows like Hand of God and Cross. The series also stars Omari Hardwick, Dana Gourrier, Kai Parham, and Amin Joseph as main characters, with Michael Ealy and Giovanni Ribisi in recurring roles.

Since his landmark gold medal win at the 1960 Olympics, Muhammad Ali has been the subject of several feature films, TV shows, animated TV series, and over a dozen major documentary films and TV specials. But the Prime Video original is special for touching upon his personal life and candid moments. The eight-episode limited series not only traces his journey from Cassius Clay to Muhammad Ali, but also looks at his life beyond the fame and fortune and depicts him as a son, brother, husband, and father.

The latest installment in the Blade Runner franchise, Blade Runner 2099 is a direct sequel to Denis Villeneuve's Blade Runner 2049 and Ridley Scott’s Blade Runner, all of which are based on Philip K. Dick's 1968 novel Do Androids Dream of Electric Sheep?. The miniseries is set 50 years after the events of the 2017 film and focuses on Olwen, an aging Replicant Blade Runner, and her mysterious partner, Cora, a human pretending to be a replicant, who are tasked with hunting down a gang of rogue Replicants. Michelle Yeoh stars as Olwen and Hunter Schafer plays Cora, with Dimitri Abold and Lewis Gribben as other main characters.

Between 1982 and now, Blade Runner has grown into a robust cyberpunk media franchise that includes two feature films, three shorts, an animated series, and several games. Created by Silka Luisa and produced by Ridley Scott, Blade Runner 2099 is one of the most anticipated sci-fi shows of 2026 and the second TV series in the titular franchise following 2021’s Blade Runner: Black Lotus. As revealed in the trailer, the upcoming miniseries shares the core atmosphere, textures, and grungy world-building established by Ridley Scott in the 1982 film but features higher stakes and a major shift in the power dynamic between humans and replicants.

Directed by Nanette Burstein, The Boy Who Stole Billions? is a deep-dive documentary series about Sam Bankman-Fried and the collapse of his $32 billion cryptocurrency empire, FTX. The three-part series delves into one of the biggest frauds in U.S. history with unprecedented access and exclusive firsthand accounts from Sam Bankman-Fried himself, provided during his prison sentence. The documentary also features never-before-seen interviews with various other figures involved with Bankman in his personal life and business, including his co-CEO and former girlfriend, Caroline Ellison.

Founded in 2019 by Bankman-Fried and Gary Wang, FTX became a major crypto exchange in its short-lived existence before collapsing in 2022 due to a major financial fraud. Bankman-Fried and his fintech empire have also been the subject of Ben MacKenzie’s 2025 documentary Everyone Is Lying to You for Money and a segment on 60 Minutes. A close-up look at the rise and fall of the poster boy of cryptocurrency, The Boy Who Stole Billions? has been described as “a series that will surprise many, bringing audiences closer to the people behind the headlines and opening up Sam's world in ways it has never been seen before.”

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: What to do with moms junker home

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Can a house that has a lien on it (from Medicaid in this case) be donated at all?
I suppose if you live in a State that does a predeath (TEFRA) lien, then no (there are 17 such states by my count that do this).

My state (and 14 others that I see online) only do liens after death. So presumably donating could be done in those states (prior to death) but again, donating a home is giving away an asset to qualify for medicaid and/or giving away an exempt asset that can later be sold to recover State used funds for the person's benefit. If one donates a home that had value it could create a period of ineligibility for medicaid while the person is alive and hoping to get or continue medicaid. Not advisable to give away assets of value. Asking for trouble in my opinion.

5 Big Challenges in Creating Criterion’s ‘The Complete Kubrick’: From Navigating HDR/4K to Preserving Stanley Kubrick’s Love of Mono Sound

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In 2024, archivist Michael Chaiken was approached by the Criterion Collection about what was being described as a “big Kubrick project.” It would start with remastering the director’s last film, “Eyes Wide Shut,” but for months the scope of the ambitious project remained an open question: Was a complete Stanley Kubrick box set even possible?

Kubrick was a precise director, and his control over every aspect of his filmmaking process extended to its home video presentation. But home video technology has developed in ways (widescreen TVs, high dynamic range, 4K resolution, Dolby Atmos) that Kubrick could never have imagined before he died in 1999. As a result — from the image to sound to design of the box itself — there was the underlying question driving Chaiken and the team’s work: “What would Stanley do?” Related Stories Ebenezer’s Venmo and ‘How to Rob a Bank’ ATMs: Studios Are Now Promoting Movies by Just Giving Away Money What Are Local Movie Theaters Showing on Election Night?

“I had to research the history of Stanley Kubrick on home video, and that’s a very specific thing,” said Chaiken. “It’s almost like for a biographer that would be [an important] footnote.”

In an interview with IndieWire, Chaiken broke down the challenges and key work behind “The Complete Kubrick,” which hit stores and mailboxes last week.

Home video fanatics are often completists. And if you are going to invest the time in a $599.95 box set (on sale for 30 percent off at most retailers), the motivation to make it comprehensive and definitive is obvious, but with a career spanning five decades, that isn’t always possible.

“You have to kick around the idea, ‘What does ‘complete’ mean? And how would we make that so?,” said Chaiken.

The heart of Kubrick’s feature film career has been well preserved. His final five films were made with Warner Bros., and the studio’s archive team has kept the original elements well preserved. Meanwhile, Criterion, back in its laser disc era, in the 1980s and ’90s, had a good working relationship with Kubrick himself (more about that later) and was already familiar with what was available (and already in-house) when it came to having to remaster his key films into the HDR/4K standards of today.

“It’s the early stuff that was tricky because he wasn’t working for a studio; he was an independent,” said Chaiken. “[A complete box set] almost didn’t happen; it was really at the 11th hour that it came together.”

In the case of “The Seafarers,” a 1953 30-minute documentary Kubrick was commissioned to make for the Seafarers International Union, the issue became untangling the project’s rights. “We thought we knew [who owned the rights], and then somebody said, ‘Hey, by the way, I’m actually the rights holder of that,’” Chaiken chuckled, retelling the story and imagining Kubrick himself groaning at the idea that Criterion had dug it up and restored it. “ I laugh because it’s a work for hire, but at the same time, it’s a very beautiful film, and it’s his first color film, and there’s elements in it that are so Kubrickian.”

The final missing piece was the original 12-minute version of the 1951 documentary “Day of the Fight,” which Kubrick shot and edited independently for Look magazine in 1949.

“There’s a version that [Kubrick] financed and made himself, and then there’s the version that exists,” said Chaiken. “He sold the film to RKO Pictures, and they re-edited the film; they added footage that he didn’t shoot, and they changed the narration and the soundtrack that Kubrick had commissioned Gerald Fried to make.”

Kubrick’s original version had never been presented in 4K, and locating the original elements was the final piece, in Chaiken’s mind, to the set being “complete.”

“Why do you need two cuts of Day of the Fight?” Chaiken rhetorically asked before answering the question: “They are different, and to reinforce that completeness, especially with [this being] Stanley’s very first film.  His cut is actually [four minutes] shorter than the one that RKO made [and] the very first film edited and produced and directed by him.”

Chaiken’s need to present both versions of a film also applied to “The Shining,” which, after it was released domestically in May 1980 — where it bombed with critics — Kubrick spent three months re-editing, making it 25 minutes shorter, for the international release in September 1980.

What’s fascinating is that both versions have been widely available for decades; they just didn’t cross the ocean. The shorter international version is Kubrick’s final cut, and what he considered his director’s cut, never made its way to the U.S.

“[Kubrick] never pulled the U.S. theatrical version, so that version is what has existed here on video. And if you’re doing complete Kubrick, the final edit of ‘The Shining’ has to be on there. It  just has to be,” said Chaiken, noting the significant differences. “The international version is a different experience. The tone of it does shift. It does leave open the possibility that none of this is happening, and it’s all happening in Jack’s head. You really don’t understand [Jack’s] relationship with Danny in the same way in the international version.”

As IndieWire’s Jim Hemphill fully explored, the significant differences between the versions are disorienting for those who grew up loving the longer U.S. version. Chaiken anticipated what “a headscratcher” the international version would be, which is why he commissioned a new commentary track. In the new box set, the U.S. version preserves steadicam operator and founder Garrett Brown’s original Criterion commentary track, but the international version features a newly recorded track by the great Pixar animation director Lee Unkrich, who co-authored a decades-in-the-making, 1,396-page, two-volume “Stanley Kubrick’s The Shining” book, which drew from rare documents, script notes, and storyboards in the Kubrick Archive, and is based on extensive interviews with the surviving cast and crew, to tell the definitive story of the legendary and misunderstood production.

“I imagine American audiences, who love the movie, saying, ‘Why would I want to watch a shorter version of ‘The Shining’? But I think having Lee Unkrich there walking you through it and talking about some of these decisions, and talking about the production of the movie, makes it a rewarding experience,” said Chaiken. “If you’re a skeptic, it’s at least worth watching once with Lee’s commentary because it is like a master-class in ‘The Shining.’”

Twenty years after Francis Ford Coppola and Walter Murch’s groundbreaking “Apocalypse Now” sound design and roadshow ushered in surround sound, and George Lucas used the box office power of his “Star Wars” franchise to leverage theaters into adapting the new technology he was pioneering at Skywalker, Kubrick was still avoiding even doing stereo mixes of his final films.

“He stuck with mono until the end,” said Chaiken. “He was obsessed with presentation, and he truly felt, ‘I would rather have good mono than bad stereo,’ and he didn’t trust that these theaters were set up in such a way that the stereo mix.”

For the box set, Criterion does offer 5.1 mixes of Kubrick’s films, but the default audio is Kubrick’s preferred mono mix. Chaiken and Criterion audio supervisor Ryan Hullings felt it important to go back to original mono mixes, which hadn’t been touched for home video in 30 years, to do new masters for the box set. In most cases, locating the original mono mix assets was not a problem, but there were exceptions, most notably “Full Metal Jacket.”

While Warner Archives had preserved all the original assets, identifying which was the final mono mix proved difficult, explained Chaiken, “ ‘Full Metal Jacket’ was a tricky one because they were trying to convince Stanley to do it in stereo. So there was the beginning of a stereo mix.” In going through what Hullings were the final mono mixes, they didn’t conform with the 35mm print he was using as a comparison. As they did throughout the process of making the box set, the Criterion team turned to Kubrick’s surviving collaborators, in this case “Full Metal Jacket” editor Martin Hunter and Nigel Galt, who would go on to edit “Eyes Wide Shut,” but had done Foley and sound work on “Full Metal Jacket.”

According to Chaiken, it was Galt who helped them solve the puzzle, telling Hullings, “You’ll know you have the right mono mix of ‘Full Metal Jacket’ because during the end credits, in the original mono theatrical mix, we screwed up.” The final song over credits is The Rolling Stones’ “Paint It Black,” but according to Galt, ‘When it went out originally theatrically, we had it at 25 frames per second, so it’s actually sped up. So if you have the mix that has ‘Paint It Black’ at 25 frames per second, that’s it.’”

And yes, on the new Criterion, the “Full Metal Jacket” mono mix preserved “Paint It Black” playing approximately four percent faster than The Rolling Stones recorded it.

“Stanley passed away in 1999, and that was the beginning of when the DVD revolution was starting to happen,” said Chaiken. “So, the idea of Blu-ray and then 4K, you’re in a realm that Kubrick could never have imagined.”

Chaiken confronted this head-on, starting the project with “Eyes Wide Shut”—which he described as one of Kubrick’s hardest films to bridge the gap between the filmmaker’s original intention and the latest in modern home video technology. In working with “Eyes Wide Shut” cinematographer Larry Smith, Chaiken came to understand how much of a “dreamy quality” is a product of the unique way Smith and Kubrick exposed and developed the celluloid.

“It’s never going to be a one-to-one correlation between film and video; you can get close, but video is getting sharper and sharper and sharper,” said Chaiken. “I saw it with ‘Eyes Wide Shut,’ that you could go very hardcore into the 4K, and you could see details in the shadows and things that Kubrick would never have wanted you to see.”

Chaiken and Criterion worked closely with the Warner Archives, Smith, and other collaborators, going back and forth with different passes to figure out how best to find a happy medium, which, with most films, meant sometimes dialing down the HDR and leaning into “more filmic, warmer curve” with the color grade.

The more Chaiken researched, the more he learned just how obsessive Kubrick was about the presentation of his films, and admitted he would get obsessive wondering if they had gotten the colors of a particular transfer right. He sought out advice from Maria Palazzola, long-time restoration supervisor, colorist, and former transfer expert at The Criterion Collection during 1980s and ‘90s, having overseen “director approved” versions of Kubrick’s Criterion laser discs.

“I had assumed that there was an intermediary, or Leon Vitali, Stanley’s longtime assistant, helping Maria,” said Chaiken.”But when I talked to her, she said, ‘No, no, Stanley was calling me directly.’”

Chaiken said he flooded Palazzola with questions trying to find out just how obsessive Kubrick was about his home video transfers and what, in particular, he obsessed about. Chaiken had heard the stories that Kubrick had made Palazzola re-transfer films countless times.

Chaiken explained his assumptions were wrong, “She said that was absolutely not the case. That  [Kubrick] understood the limitations of the technology at that time, and he wanted them to look as good as they could possibly look on home video.”

For example, according Palazzola told Chaiken with “2001: A Space Odyssey,” Kubrick only pressed hard on two key sections.

“He wanted to be sure that the Dawn of Man sequence — that was front projected: The backgrounds were projected when they were filming it — and so he wanted to make sure that you couldn’t see any reflection in the eyes of the apes from that front projection,” said Chaiken. “And she said the one reel that he made her re-transfer a few times was the last reel, in the French boudoir, because [Kubrick] didn’t want it to be too bright.”

Chaiken said the anecdotes of Kubrick’s collaboration with Palazzola changed his perspective on his own work: “Hearing that he was relatively easy to work with was a bit of a relief because he cared about this stuff immensely, but he understood that film is not video. It’s a completely different medium.”

Chaiken gives full credit to visual artist Drusilla Adeline (aka Sister Hyde) for the design of the Kubrick box set, and in this video, you can hear them discuss how the fonts, color, layout, and the box itself were built around the theme of “Kubrick at work,” and how, in going through his archive, you get a sense of him leaving his fingerprints (literally and figuratively) on the making of his films.

One of the things that is striking about the box set, loaded with 16 different films, extras, and supporting materials, is how — compared to most box sets — it is laid out in a way that it is very easy to find what you are looking for, but also clear explanations for each film (each separated by a tab and film poster) of what is versions, audio, and extras are available. Chaiken said that in going through Kubrick’s work, it was clear what an organized and clear archivist the filmmaker was himself.

“In my mind, I was thinking about the Library of America —  what would a Library of America compendium of Stanley Kubrick look like? That  was definitely a touchstone,” said Chaiken. “Library of America editions, where they’ll [be] a single compendium of a great American author and you have the major works, but then you also have these interesting tangential pieces, and they’re laid out so beautifully, they’re just so uniform. They have the little tab that marks a page.”

Strand bookstore to pay nearly $1.5 million over Covid loan tied to Sen. Wyden's wife

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Strand, the iconic New York City bookstore, agreed to pay nearly $1.5 million in a civil settlement for accepting a Covid government loan that was off-limits because the store is owned by the wife of Oregon Democratic Sen. Ron Wyden, according to the U.S. Attorney's Office in Washington, D.C.

The Strand applied for the $932,350 loan in February 2021 from the Paycheck Protection Program administered by the Small Business Administration, U.S. Attorney Jeanine Pirro's office said Tuesday.

Store owner Nancy Bass Wyden certified on the loan application that "the spouse of a Member of Congress does not directly or indirectly hold a controlling interest in the applicant," Pirro's office said.

"Ms. Wyden's certification was knowingly and materially false and Strand was not eligible for the second draw loan it received," the office said.

The Strand will pay $1,482,560 to settle the case. There was no determination of liability by the store, Pirro's office said.

The Strand, in a statement, said the store made an "unintentional" mistake in applying for the loan, and said it was unaware at the time the funds were barred to businesses owned by the spouse of a member of Congress.

Bass Wyden's husband, who has served in the Senate for more than 30 years, is a staunch critic of President Donald Trump, who appointed Pirro to the post of top prosecutor in the District of Columbia.

Bass Wyden is the granddaughter of Ben Bass, founder of the Strand, whose flagship store is in Greenwich Village.

Congress authorized the forgivable PPP loans when it passed the Coronavirus Aid, Relief, and Economic Security Act in March 2020.

In December 2020, Congress provided additional funding for the PPP loan program with separate legislation, which "included a prohibition against businesses obtaining second draw PPP funds if a member of Congress or their spouse directly or indirectly held a controlling interest in the PPP loan applicant," Pirro's office said.

In a statement Tuesday, Pirro said, "The Paycheck Protection Program was created to save American jobs, not to enrich those who aren't eligible for taxpayer-funded relief, especially the wife of a U.S. Senator."

Her office noted that the False Claims Act, which the Strand was alleged to have violated, "allows the government to establish liability based on reckless disregard and permits the government to recover more than its actual losses," which is what occurred in this case.

The civil settlement resolved claims brought under a so-called qui tam lawsuit, which allows private whistleblowers to sue on behalf of the U.S. government and receive a portion of any recovery.

The qui tam action in this case was filed by a corporate entity, Blockquote Inc., in U.S. District Court in Washington. Blockquote, whose owners were not disclosed by Pirro's office, will receive a total share of about $148,256 in connection with the settlement.

The docket for Blockquote's lawsuit is sealed from public view.

The Strand, in a statement after CNBC requested comment from the store and Bass Wyden, said the restriction that the store violated was passed by Congress between the first and second rounds of the PPP.

"Unfortunately, the bookstore's accountant and the bank administering the loan were not aware of this change when the second-draw application was processed," the Strand said.

"We stand by the facts: the circumstances arose during an unprecedented and rapidly changing public health emergency, the mistake was unintentional," the store said. "We hope this matter will be viewed objectively and in its proper context, with the understanding that the application of justice should always be fair, impartial, and free from political considerations," the store said.

Sen. Wyden's office did not immediately respond to requests for comment.

WASHINGTON, Nov. 3, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- I AM ALS and its national force of advocates are celebrating as the President signed the ACT for ALS reauthorization bill into law. This comes after the Senate approved the bill by unanimous consent with just hours left until the law expired, even after the House went home early. The I AM ALS community first identified a major gap in access to investigational treatments and co-authored the original ACT for ALS Act to address it in 2021. Now, with years of success underway, the law has five more years to scale research infrastructure and treatment access, representing an investment of up to an additional $500 million for ALS.

"Over the past 7+ years, this community has changed what's possible in ALS," said I AM ALS co-founder Brian Wallach. "The I AM ALS community's leadership has brought us closer than ever to a cure by unlocking over $2 billion for federal ALS research, expanding and protecting access to life-changing therapies, and accelerating scientific breakthroughs. Unlike any other legislation the neurodegenerative landscape has ever seen, ACT for ALS has already invested hundreds of millions of dollars into ALS research, provided investigational therapy access for more than 800 patients who would not otherwise have qualified, and built lasting infrastructure for future learnings.

"These past months didn't just get a critical bill passed; they inspired a community that continually renews itself, welcomes new advocates, develops new leaders, and ensures that every generation is ready to fight for the next breakthrough," said Troy Fields, volunteer co-chair of the I AM ALS legislative affairs team, and person living with ALS. Valve is making changes to deal with a ‘significant increase’ in Steam game submissions

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Valve admits that it’s now taking ‘up to 12 business days’ to review games.

Valve admits that it’s now taking ‘up to 12 business days’ to review games.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

In a new post today, Valve acknowledged that it’s seen “a significant increase in submissions for store and page review in recent months” and says it’s “running a little behind” by taking “up to 12 business days” for reviews instead of the typical 3-5 days.

Indie developers have recently expressed frustrations over lengthy approval times for Steam games, even causing some games to miss planned launch dates. Valve says that “much” of the delay is having to re-review games, so it’s making some changes to the release checklist for developers to hopefully “cut down on re-reviews for basic functional issues.”

The changes include requiring developers to run games through Steam for at least five minutes. “Believe it or not, sometimes we review builds that have not ever been played through Steam,” Valve says. “This means that even if a game runs smoothly when a dev launches their executable directly, they might have missed basic steps that would impact a user installing and playing via Steam.”

Other requirements include making sure that achievements are ready and confirming any required common redistributables, like DirectX or Visual C++, that Steam may need to install.

Valve also says more improvements are in the works for the build review process.

$12,000 Buffalo–New York Fares Making Headlines—I’m Finding Nonstops For $337 Roundtrip

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A $12,203 airfare between Buffalo and New York about 300 miles each way – is going viral as evidence of how expensive it’s gotten to fly home for the holidays. The TikTok video that started it off though shows a roundtrip price (not one-way) and connecting both directions in Florida (not non-stop). Meanwhile, nonstops on that route seem to start at $337 roundtrip. Kristen Mirand says she was trying to get home to Buffalo and received a notification for a $200 flight from New York. Then she looked at returning, and found five-figure prices with connections in Orlando and Fort Lauderdale. She joked that she’d start walking. The New York Post ran with “Flight from Buffalo to NYC reportedly reached $12K — as holiday airfare hits an all-time high” and suggests higher fuel costs and holiday demand are involved here. ZeroHedge describes a “roughly 90 minute flight,” and launches into airlines keeping fares high after fuel prices fall. It ends with the prospect of airlines “laughing all the way to the bank.” Look at the flights actually displayed in the video: $11,400 roundtrip, returning through Orlando. These are 7 and 9 hour trips from Buffalo to JFK. $12,203 roundtrip, returning through Fort Lauderdale. These are 7 and 8.5 hour trips. Every price is labeled round trip. you’re seeing the cost of the whole ticket when you select a return. This wasn’t a $12,000 nonstop and none of these itineraries gets you there in 90 minutes. I’m seeing nonstop roundtrips around $337 on Delta Air Lines, JetBlue, United Airlines and American Airlines, checking across the New York airports. Even if you want to connect in Florida, Frontier Airlines can be as low as $210 roundtrip. She doesn’t show her dates, but at those prices be flexible! My guess is that the pricing is breaking somewhere as the system puts together this unnatural itinerary. A fare between Buffalo and New York comes with rules about how you can travel and Florida connections aren’t usually included in the routing rules. A search can wind up combining separate fares for different pieces of the trip instead of using the simple fare between the two cities. Bad fare combining or an error putting together the outbound and return can cause weird outcomes. While the last seats on a flight can be expensive, “last seat available” doesn’t seem like the explanation for a $12,000 Buffalo–New York price. Airfares are up. Hopper’s says Thanksgiving domestic roundtrips average $402, up 31%, and Christmas $452, up 23%. With millions of fare quotes being generated, even a tiny error rate is going to produce outlier results. Mistake fares can be fares that are too high, not just too low. Faced with a fare like this, I’d: Search for nonstops on the date I actually want. Start with the trip I’d choose to take before considering a seven-hour detour. Check JFK, LaGuardia and Newark. Restricting the search to one airport leaves out alternatives that may work just as well. Search the airline websites directly. See whether the airline produces the same price, rather than treating one Google Flights result as the answer. Start a fresh search and compare one-ways with the roundtrip. Check the dates, cabin, passenger count and currency. Separate searches can get you away from whatever combination generated the nonsense price. For this route I’d also price Amtrak’s Empire Service between Buffalo and New York. Those Florida connections already consume most of the day before you add getting to the airport, security and the trip into Manhattan. BitMine Reports 6.06M ETH Holdings, With More Than 5.22M Staked

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: BitMine Immersion Technologies reported holdings of 6,060,506 ETH as of November 1 in a November 2 filing exhibit. Cryptocurrency balance sheets are sensitive to valuation dates.

The filing’s detailed token count is valuable precisely because it can be compared with later disclosures. It also exposes a blind spot in quick “ETH treasury” comparisons: two companies with identical token balances could have very different liquidity, financing and operational risk. Staking arrangements are an essential part of that distinction.

BitMine Immersion Technologies reported holdings of 6,060,506 ETH as of November 1 in a November 2 filing exhibit. The company said 5,227,037 ETH were staked, alongside smaller Bitcoin holdings, cash and other positions. These are issuer-disclosed balances at a specified date, not independently observed live holdings or a claim about Ethereum’s total currently liquid supply. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Staking means committing ETH to help secure Ethereum’s proof-of-stake network through validators or staking arrangements. Rewards depend on protocol conditions and operating choices, while access to staked assets can involve withdrawal queues or third-party arrangements. A substantial staked position may produce ongoing reward income, but it is not equivalent to holding the same amount in immediately transferable cash.

BitMine’s size makes its liquidity choices important. A large corporate ETH owner may hold a long-term position, but the market will still examine potential issuance, borrowing, staking income and the ability to rebalance. The issuer says its holdings represent a notable share of ETH’s supply; the comparison is a company calculation and should be tied to the supply and price assumptions used.

Ethereum has multiple sources of demand and risk: trading, staking, stablecoin settlement, application fees and long-term treasury allocation. A change in one part of that system should be tested against the others instead of promoted into a universal explanation. The market often overreacts when one publicly available metric is mistaken for the whole network economy.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on BitMine’s earlier ETH accumulation and staking disclosure and our Ethereum market reporting. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Cryptocurrency balance sheets are sensitive to valuation dates. A dollar figure derived from the company’s ETH price assumption can change materially without any coins moving. Staking rewards should also be separated from share-price performance and company operating costs. A large token position does not by itself tell shareholders whether their ownership is increasing or being diluted.

For investors, the right comparison is between liquidity, expected holding period and obligations. Staked ETH, ETF shares and treasury holdings may all track the same underlying asset, but their exit routes and operating constraints differ. A useful report therefore asks not simply how much ETH is involved, but how readily the exposure can be changed when conditions deteriorate.

Look for future reports distinguishing unstaked liquidity, locked or queued balances, reward income, cash uses and equity issuance. Those measures will tell investors more about treasury resilience than the headline ETH number alone. For Ethereum, the interesting network question is whether institutional-sized staking concentrations affect how observers assess operator diversity and risk.
